- **2026-10-10 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî COMPLETED ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] classifyError: one error order for every input shape (board `c60f5b45`, PR #1057, branch `claude/classifier-order`).**  Cause: `classifyError` checked transient before terminal for a CLI exit report but terminal before transient for an Error or text, and `unexpected status` sat in the terminal invalid-request arm whatever the digits, so `unexpected status 503` and `429` classified as terminal, a monthly rate cap was retried, `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED ... capacity` read as overloaded, and a stopped turn could come back as an auto-retry.  Fix: one order for every shape, auth and quota and the user-stop phrases first, then the transient vocabulary, then the remaining terminal arms, then the HTTP status digits.  Bare `interrupted` no longer means the user stopped the turn, only `interrupted by user` and `cancelled by user` do.  `rate limit reached` is quota only with a monthly, billing-cycle, plan or hard-cap cue in the same clause, so an OpenAI per-minute TPM 429 ("try again in 6s") stays a retryable `rate_limited`.  Replaces the earlier shape-independence change on main (93b3b20de): a CLI exit whose stderr matches nothing now reads `unknown` like the other two shapes, and `terminal_exit` is kept for a nonzero exit with no text at all.  Tests: `server/drivers/retry.test.ts` shape-invariance table plus the TPM, monthly-cap, user-stop and signal-close cases; the codex, claude, antigravity, acp and chat-completions suites pass.
- **2026-10-10 - MM - COMPLETED - Chat UI libraries: assistant-ui (macOS) + Exyte Chat (iOS).** Claimed Sat, Oct 10, 2026 ¬∑ board `2b6d247c`. Owner chose assistant-ui for Mac and Exyte Chat for iOS.  Verified by building the assistant-ui adapter in-lane against the harness stream (A/B-verified, typechecks clean; existing ChatView/ChatMarkdown 23/23 still pass).  Found: `@assistant-ui/react-ui` is broken upstream (imports `useAssistantRuntime`, which no published `@assistant-ui/react` exports) so we style on `ThreadPrimitive` directly.  Corrected the 2026-09-30 audit: Exyte is NOT in the repo, and the Mac app is Electron+React not `mac/Sources/`.  iOS recommendation is borrow-patterns, not full swap (UIList is a wrapped UITableView; Glass + 615 lines of custom cards would lose native SwiftUI).  Decision doc: `docs/decisions/2026-10-10-chat-ui-libraries.md`.  Review round: Kody flagged a vacuous render test, a dropped pre-settle stream, and a reasoning-only isRunning bug; all three fixed and A/B-verified.  One review suggestion (guard isRunning on last.role === assistant) was rejected as wrong after the synthetic-message fix and pinned by a test.  Lint ratchet failure fixed by DELETING three type assertions rather than documenting them (anti-slop/require-safety-comment-for-type-assertion 1556 vs 1557 baseline; the summary line read DOWN at 4874/4890 while the per-rule check failed).  Autofix [4/5] correct and kept with tests (onReload must forward sourceId + runConfig); [3/5] rejected as stale and pinned by a test.  Autofix [5/5] correct (synthetic streaming bubble must sort strictly after the triggering message, not tie with it); the first test for it asserted >= and was vacuous - the A/B check caught it.  Final: 10/10 adapter tests, full src suite 191 files / 2424 tests, lint ratchet passed (4873 vs 4890), zero type assertions in the adapter.  PR #1054.
- **2026-10-10 ‚Äî CLUTCH ‚Äî IN PROGRESS ‚Äî [BF][CLUTCH] Platform marks stop taking the theme accent, and the Clutch engine reads DeepSeek-V4-Pro instead of a Pro row DeepSeek never published (board `915ab05a`, branch `clutch/platform-marks-and-model-names`, lane `~/apps/lanes/BotFleet/clutch-platform-marks-and-model-names`).**  Owner, two reports in one screenshot: the engine rail's platform logos were painted in the accent colour, and the Clutch picker listed `DeepSeek-V4.1-Pro`.  **Marks.**  Two separate causes, neither visible to the type checker.  Grok, OpenAI and OpenCode painted `fill-[var(--color-ink)]`, so a palette that puts main text on the accent paints those logos in the accent; DeepSeek (`#4D6BFE`), Claude (`#D97757`) and the Gemini, Qwen and MiniMax marks carried their brand's paint outright, and the gradients were three or four pixels of mud at 14 to 18px.  One `--color-mark` token now carries every mark: declared in each of the ten preset skins next to that skin's own ink, and set for the Custom Palette from the ground's lightness (`#2b3038` light, `#d8dbe0` dark), never from the palette's ink or accent, so a palette cannot recolor a logo.  The shapes stay, because the shape is what tells one engine from another; Cursor stays the monochrome PNG it already was.  **Model.**  DeepSeek's own catalog (api-docs.deepseek.com, verified 2026-10-10) is wire id `deepseek-flash` / version `DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash`, plus wire id `deepseek-v4-pro` / version `DeepSeek-V4-Pro-0813`.  Only the Flash row carries a ".1", so the picker's Pro row was a name DeepSeek never published; it reads `DeepSeek-V4-Pro`, the old id folds onto it through `RETIRED_MODEL_REPLACEMENTS`, and a stale `DeepSeek-V4.1-Pro` display name in an owner's `settings.yaml` folds onto the same row instead of appearing twice.  The two DeepSeek ids the vision route keys off are now exported constants (`DSH_DEEPSEEK_PRO_MODEL`, `DSH_DEEPSEEK_FLASH_MODEL`) instead of three copies of a string literal, because that literal is exactly what went stale: the send path in `index.ts` now asks `dshVisionSelection` which ids count as Pro, so the two cannot disagree, and the retired spelling still routes off Pro while a rewrite is pending.  Prices were already right and are untouched.  Tests: `ProviderIcons.test.tsx` renders every driver kind and fails on any inline fill, gradient reference, custom property or accent spelling; `skins-mark.test.ts` applies real palettes in happy-dom and fails if a palette's mark tone equals its accent or ink, or if switching to a preset skin leaves the palette's mark behind; `tests/e2e/engine-marks.spec.ts` mounts the real rail in Atelier, Midnight and the palette whose main text is its accent, and asserts the resolved colour of every painted mark.  Mutation-checked: reverting one mark to `fill-[var(--color-ink)]` fails the hostile-palette spec and nothing else, which is the shape of the original bug.  No `toHaveScreenshot` in that spec on purpose: this directory's baselines are `-chromium-linux.png` renders made with pinned DejaVu fonts, and this Mac has no DejaVu, so a Mac baseline can never match CI's.  Not done: no GitHub issue or PR.  This session has no working GitHub credential (gh token invalid, keychain empty), so the branch cannot be pushed from it.

- **2026-10-10 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Shared VPS image rebuild: build the new CUA image ahead while the container runs, then swap in a restart, and stop calling a stale-image container "Running" (board `652521ac`, branch `claude/vps-image-rebuild`).**  Cause: `prepareVpsImage` ran only from provision with no container, so every `IMAGE_LAYER_VERSION` bump left the shared container on an old image that bots refuse, and the only way out was remove then provision, a 20 to 45 minute outage.  Fix: `POST /api/vps-computer/prepare-image` builds the pinned image in the background, single-flight per VPS host (a provision that needs the image joins it), with `imageBuild` (idle, building with elapsed time, failed with reason, ready) and `imageOutdated` in `GET /api/vps-computer`; `POST /api/vps-computer/switch-image` replaces the container from the prepared image under the lifecycle lock (a restart, not a rebuild), refused while the image is missing or building or a bot holds the shared VPS.  The card says "Running (outdated image)", offers Prepare Image, shows build progress, then Switch to New Image with a confirm that the container filesystem resets.  Auto-starting the build is deliberately not included.  Tests: server build state machine, single-flight, swap refusals and swap; card labels and rendered states; three new Playwright baselines.
- **2026-10-09 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] iOS TestFlight: builds shipped green to the new app record but reached nobody (board `acb2c8a9`, GH #1018, branch `claude/ios-ship-bundle-id`).**  Owner report: nothing in TestFlight since the bundle ID change.  Runs: red Sep 25 to Oct 2 (ASC credential read, fixed in #804) and on Oct 7 (#918), green since #926, but every green ship landed on `app.botfleet.ios` (6820175685), which had no internal TestFlight group (the legacy record had `BotFleet Testers`), while the external Public Beta testers stayed NOT_INVITED because Beta App Review submission got a 422 on every ship.  14 VALID builds, none installable.  Fix: `ensure-standing-testers` now creates or reuses an all-builds internal group and adds the standing emails that are App Store Connect users; tester records are per app, so the add creates the record instead of reusing the first one found by email (that reuse answered 409); `ensure-tf-ready` exits 5 and the ship warns when no internal tester exists, instead of claiming internal testers can install.  Applied live to App Store Connect the same day: group `Internal Testers` with the two ASC users, and build 202610091444 submitted for Beta App Review.  Tests: 16 in `scripts/ios-ship-workflow.node-test.mjs`.
- **2026-10-09 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Kody sweep, high tier: Approve All counts what settled, the CLI wrapper stops going offline when Bypass is ticked, and the lease queue stops blocking the event loop (board `603bd6ae`, issue 1008, branch `claude/kody-high-server`).**  Four merged-PR findings fixed and one left open, each confirmed on main.  PR 870: `approvedCount` in Approve All was bumped for every routine card, including one whose subject changed after it was shown (it comes back `invalid`, stays pending with `held` set and still owns the composer), so the operator was told the backlog was clear; it now counts only a card that applied, through `routineApprovalApplied`.  PR 826: Kody's `.strict()` is in the CLI wrapper schema, but the Engines page writes `fullAuto` and `cli` and the credential flow writes `key` and `credentialStorage` into ANY driver's config, so ticking Bypass permissions on a wrapped CLI made `decodeConfig` throw and the registry turn the instance into a shadow entry that took the engine offline.  The schema now accepts and drops those four harness-owned keys and still rejects every other unknown key, so a typo like `commmand` cannot quietly run the default `echo` (this walks back part of what Kody asked for, on purpose).  PR 864 is NOT fixed here and is left for an owner decision: the Muse install command is still an unpinned `npm install -g @bex-co/muse-code-acp`, but `0.7.0` (the release the driver was written against) bundles `@muse-code/sdk` 1.3.0, which `server/drivers/muse-msp-bridge.ts` documents as not working against Muse Code 1.4.2, and `0.9.0` (bundling 1.4.4) is under a day old and untested here, so a pin would move every new install onto a version nobody has shown works; the choice is to pin a version proven against current Muse, or wire the bridge.  PR 848: acquire and release ran `existsSync`, `mkdirSync` and `rmSync` inside the per-repository queue; they are `fs/promises` calls now, and `server/worktree-leases-async-fs.test.ts` fails on the old code because a synchronous `mkdirSync` or `rmSync` throws while it runs a real acquire, a leftover-directory re-acquire and a release.  PR 797: the Windows skip of the symlink suites now says why (comment only).  Not changed: `pruneStaleWorktrees` still scans with sync calls, outside the queue.
- **2026-10-09 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Updater: install the newest commit with a green hosted build instead of failing on a busy main (branch `claude/updater-newest-green-build`).**  Cause: `ubf` with no `--target` insisted on origin/main's tip, and every push to main cancels the superseded "Mac Commit Build", so the tip's build is usually queued or cancelled.  On Oct 9 a green build existed at bd86b046a at 6:47am, PR #967 merged five minutes later (c5b644b74), its build was cancelled, and the 6:52am update failed with "did not succeed (cancelled)".  Now `scripts/update-botfleet.sh` asks `selectUpdateTarget` in `scripts/ci-build-resolver.mjs` for the newest first-parent commit on main, newer than the installed build identity, whose hosted build succeeded and whose artifact is still there (one Actions listing, one artifact lookup), prints "Updating to <sha> (main is N commits ahead; its build is still running)", and bootstraps the updater from THAT commit, so the policy that judges a build is the policy of the commit being installed; the chosen commit meets the same origin/main ancestry rule as any target.  An explicit `--target`, `BOTFLEET_UPDATE_TARGET`, apply, unquiesce, `--source`/`--bundle` and `BOTFLEET_UPDATE_SOURCE=local` skip the selection entirely, and every doubt (no network, no installed-build record, an installed commit this checkout cannot place on main, a resolver without the export) falls through to the tip as before.  When nothing newer is green the wrapper hands the explanation to the tip's updater (`BOTFLEET_UPDATE_SELECTION_FAILURE`) instead of ending the run, so it lands in the progress record rather than reading as "never started".  Tests: tip green, tip cancelled with an earlier green commit, tip running with nothing newer, never the installed build, explicit target unchanged, plus the wrapper and progress-record paths.  Same PR, coordinator ask the same morning: a slow boot is no longer a failed update.  A forced update on the owner's Mac installed the new build, the new harness logged "booting" and was still starting up when the 90-second window closed (load averages of 150 to 900), so `verifyHarness` failed and the update rolled back, and the restored harness then needed about two minutes of its own.  `startupTimeoutMs` now defaults to 15 minutes (`DEFAULT_STARTUP_TIMEOUT_MS`, `BOTFLEET_STARTUP_TIMEOUT_MS` still overrides), `verifyHarness` and the rollback restart wait share `waitForStartup`, which reports "Waiting for the new version to start, 3 min so far" once a minute into the progress record and ends early only on a real crash (the launchd job has exited or been killed by a signal, or launchd has had to start it again), and the app wait fails fast if the app was running and is gone.  The newest-green selection lives in the wrapper, so the machine copy of the updater on the owner's Mac picks that part up only after it is refreshed from the repo; the start-up wait lives in the updater the wrapper bootstraps from the chosen commit and needs nothing.
- **2026-10-09 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Auto-review follow-ups to #996: an always-allow is reviewed on a held turn, queued steps are reviewed after a turn ends, and every writing `agents` tool is watched (issue #1006, branch `claude/auto-review-followups`, after #996 landed as `d0730fc2b`).**  A read-only review of #996 (head `6f0452978`) found three P2 gaps and three P3 ones.  (1) On reviewed less than Watch for a person's always-allow on a held turn: `markAsked` drops the step from the watch the moment the ask reaches the card, and an always-allow answers at the card with no review.  `reviewsGrant` now takes `heldTurn`, and under On on a held turn an always-allow goes through the same reviewer screen as an Auto or Bypass grant, with its own held-card wording.  Off a held turn, and under Watch, it is still the person's decision.  (2) `turnEnded` deleted the queue, so a Box burst of `item.started` followed by `turn.completed` lost every step but the first with no row and no flag.  Queued steps are now kept and reviewed late: a refusal is flagged after the turn ended, a step nobody could check is a `review-skipped` row plus an On chip, none of them can stop a turn (they are marked by step identity, the step already under review or waiting out its grace included, so the next turn on an engine with no turn id is safe), they skip the ask grace, and they spend a snapshot of what the turn had left of its limit (`ReviewBudget.remaining`) instead of a released budget.  (3) Only `ask_bot` and `delegate_bot` were picked out by name, so `job_start`, `zulip_post`, `zulip_reply`, `create_bot`, `request_credential`, `job_kill` and `propose_routine*` (all `task` on Claude, because the server is called `agents`) were never watched, though claude.ts and the PR said they were.  The watch now derives the set from the registry (every MCP tool marked `write`), so a new one is covered, and `job_start` is watched but never carded.  (4) The by-name match normalises `agents.x`, `agents/x`, `mcp:agents/x`, `mcp_agents_x` and ACP title forms; this repo holds no captured ACP MCP frame, so those are the spellings engines are known to use, not ones read from a fixture.  (5) The held-turn summary says nothing already started is undone.  (6) The step watch has no `local-computer` check and needs none, since it can only record or stop; a test pins it.  Also one `// SAFETY:` comment in `src/lib/bot-settings-gates.test.ts`: main itself exceeded the type-assertion lint baseline (1558 against 1557 measured on an export of `origin/main`), which fails the lint check for any PR, and this branch adds no warning of its own.  Verified: the new tests fail against #996's head and pass here; `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, 17 targeted files (910 tests) and `git diff --check` clean.  Full `pnpm test` on the merged tree: 9875 passed, 6 failed in 4 files: `server/env-path.test.ts` (canonical mcode) fails the same way on main's sources, and the three webhook tests (`unattended`, `http-lane-e2e`, `decision-log-wiring`) timed out at load 50+ and pass alone on this branch (A/B against main's sources: they pass there too).
- **2026-10-09 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR (PR #1020) ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Kanban Command Center stops queuing every retained routine run: acknowledged failures are skipped, a routine's repeats fold into one card, and every column is paged (board `fbd2be5e`, branch `claude/kanban-card-bounds`, lane `~/apps/lanes/botfleet/claude-kanban-card-bounds`).**  Owner screenshot:  1609 cards and 449 Needs Action, the Attention Queue full of identical months-old "Run Failed" cards for one routine whose cause (#959) was already fixed.  Cause in `src/components/KanbanCommandCenter.tsx` (#851):  one card per non-cancelled run of a visible bot, from `/api/routines`, which returns every retained run (2,000 terminal runs plus all active ones);  `seenAt` was never read, so failures acknowledged on the Routines page kept queuing;  only Completed was capped.  Now:  failed and missed runs that `needsAttention` no longer counts (the shared `src/lib/routine-attention.ts` definition the badge and the server's Acknowledge All use) are skipped;  a routine's failed and missed runs fold into one card for the newest run with a "+N older" count, a pending approval keeps its own card, and webhook and resource triggers are keyed by `triggerAttentionKey` so they never merge with a routine;  every column shows 15 cards with a "Show N More" control, and the column badge and header strip keep the true totals.  The per-card click, the filter box and the ranking (unblock value, then wait time) are unchanged.  Tests:  `KanbanCommandCenter.test.tsx` (acknowledged, collapse, cap, nothing new under the cap), `KanbanCommandCenter.paging.test.tsx` (clicks, happy-dom), and `tests/e2e/kanban-command-center.spec.ts` on a new `flood` fixture state (counts and text, no new pixel baseline);  each new behavior was mutation-checked.  The six existing Kanban and Fleet Matrix visual specs pass against their Linux baselines in the cached `bf-playwright:1.63-dejavu` container, but they are insensitive (a cap of 1 also passed them), so the unit and behavior tests are the real guard.
- **2026-10-09 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Updates: tell people their messages are held, and drop the frozen percent (PR #991, follow-up to #960, branch `claude/update-drain-visibility`, closes review threads 4227614183, 4227618055 and 4227618394; issue #982).**  A send accepted during an update's hold was kept without a word: the bot looked stuck and a room went quiet.  `GET /api/runtime` reports the hold but needs the harness owner's token on a loopback address, so the app and the paired phone could not read it, and it is not on the companion allowlist.  The hold now rides `UpdateStatus.drain` on `GET /api/update/status` and the `update.status` push (the phone already reads both, so no new route): counts and times, with `windowEndsAt` as the ceiling on the wait.  `UpdateDrain` gained `onChange`, and the harness broadcasts when the hold begins, renews or ends and every two seconds while it lasts, so an updater started from a terminal shows too.  Desktop: a line above the composer in every chat and room, a line on the floating card, the Settings Updates card and the sidebar button, and the queued chip says the message waits for the restart rather than for an idle bot.  iOS: the same line above the composer and under the Mac Update card.  The step percent was the run's own step count, frozen during the grace, so "Waiting for 3 bots to finish (40%)" was a number the wait never reached: the harness now withholds `progress` while a `detail` is set, and the desktop label, the floating card's bar and the phone's bar drop it by the same rule.  The third thread was a test fixture with two ASCII spaces where the harness sends U+00A0 plus a space; that fixture only feeds `installPausesWork`, so no assertion changed.  Verified: `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, the targeted vitest files (including the hold test in `server/index.test.ts`), `swift test --filter MacUpdate`, and every `pnpm test` suite.  This Mac was at a load average of 300 to 860, so the full run failed 7 tests and the `comms` e2e suite (`comms`, `decision-log-wiring`, `env-path`, `http-lane-e2e`, `index` (deferred fallback ownership) and `unattended`), plus 2 shell-wrapper tests in `update-botfleet-mac.node-test.mjs`.  The same ones fail at origin/main (A/B; the `index` suite did not even come up there), and none touch this change.
- **2026-10-09 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR (PR #1026) ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Fleet stat pills count each bot once, not once per room it belongs to (board `710979ea`, branch `claude/fleet-pill-distinct-bots`).**  Owner screenshot: the Fleet Matrix header read 10 Errors, 10 Working and 18 Unread while the Kanban board said 1 In Progress.  Cause: `summarizeFleetAttention` in `src/lib/attention-index.ts` summed the per-room counts, and every room copies a bot's one global activity and unread flag, so a bot in N rooms counted N times.  Fix: the fleet rollup (Fleet Matrix header and the All Apps tab) counts distinct bots for Errors, Needs Action and Working, and Unread counts each unread room chat once plus each unread member bot once; `RoomAttention.unread` gains `roomUnread` and `bots`.  Per-room chips and matrix rows are unchanged, and per-(bot, room) state (#815) stays out of scope.  Verified: six new vitest cases (four unit, two component) fail on the old source and pass now; typecheck, the lint ratchet and the PR lint gate are clean; `vitest related` on the three changed modules passes (4 files, 19 tests).  The full vitest run had 33 failures, all in nine `server/*` harness files that import nothing this PR changes (four pass when run alone, the rest fail the same way in a sibling lane).  The fleet-matrix Linux baselines still show "2 Working" for the fixture: one digit, inside the 2 percent tolerance, so not regenerated.
- **2026-10-09 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Kody sweep, visual coverage: the held-bots and short-fallback-chain notices and the Permission Bypass dialog now have Playwright baselines (board `60462e50`, branch `claude/kody-critical-visual-coverage`).**  Two critical Kody findings still on main: PR #763 (the Engine Quotas notices in `UsageSection`) and PR #870 (`BypassPermissionsWarning`) shipped web UI with no screenshot coverage.  BotFleet has no top-level `visual-tests/` directory, so this follows the repo's real convention: `tests/e2e/*.visual.spec.ts` with a `/?fixture=` harness in `src/main.tsx`, baselines under `tests/e2e/<spec>-snapshots/`.  The two notices move out of `UsageSection.tsx` into `src/components/UsageNotices.tsx` with identical markup (the section renders them, a fixture mounts them with fixed data); new specs `usage-notices.visual.spec.ts` (plural and singular headings) and `bypass-warning.visual.spec.ts` (standard model, lightweight model, busy). - **2026-10-08 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Local VM: a slow Docker is no longer "Start docker first", and the image verifier checks ping by presence (branch `claude/local-vm-docker-check-and-ping`).**  Causes: `containerRuntimeStatus` probed `docker info` once with a 10 second timeout and read a timeout as "daemon down", so on a Mac at load 115-450 a GET showed `daemonUp: true` and seconds later POST /run said "Start docker first" with OrbStack up the whole time;  and the baked `botfleet-vm-cli-verify` ran `ping -V`, but the running VM drops every capability except SETUID and SETGID, ping carries `cap_net_raw`, and Linux then refuses to exec it at all, so the verifier exited 1 with "missing VM CLIs: ping" as cua while the root build-time verify passed.  The probe now makes two 12 second attempts and retries only a timeout (a fast "Cannot connect" is one call, a healthy daemon is one call);  two timeouts report a new `daemonSlow` status and "Docker is slow to respond right now; try again in a moment" instead of "Start docker first", which also reaches the lifecycle actions, the bot-turn wake, and the card (header, Step 2 and Step 4 now read `daemonSlow`, and the card's status timeout is 40 seconds so it outlasts the probe).  ping is verified with `command -v ping`, no capability added;  strace -V works under the dropped caps and keeps its real check, so ping is the only tool changed.  `IMAGE_LAYER_VERSION` stays at 8 on purpose (coordinator ruling):  a bump for a verify-only tweak would block Local VM bots until Prepare and a recreate and invalidate Self-Hosted VPS containers, so the new check reaches the next image built for a real reason.  Reproduced in a throwaway container from the v8 image with the VM's cap flags (not the live container).  Not changed: the 4 second `which` presence check can still read a timeout as "Install a supported container runtime first".

- **2026-10-09 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Kody sweep, high tier: opening a bot in an App no longer drops the App after the task switch, a frame no longer closes the matrix overview, and the plugins view derives its types from its schemas (board `603bd6ae`, issue 1010, branch `claude/kody-high-client`).**  Three merged-PR findings.  PR 854, confirmed by reading the code paths and not by running the app: `openBotInApp` selects a bot with the pin set to the App's thread, then `switchTask`; the `taskSwitched` ack clears the pin for the selected bot on purpose; the effect in `src/App.tsx` read that null pin as leaving the App and cleared `selectedAppId`, and `appKeyboardRouting` also needed a truthy pin, so the highlight and the app-scoped shortcuts were lost as soon as the switch landed.  The same effect called `setMatrixOverviewActive(false)` on every run, and its dependencies include `state.bots` and `state.groups`, which change identity on every SSE frame, so the overview the user opened closed on the next frame.  The decision is now `resolveAppContext` in `src/lib/task-app-thread.ts` (a new selection counts only when it pinned the App's thread; the same selection after an ack or a frame reads the bot's active thread; the matrix yields only when the selection changed), and the keyboard routing uses `shownThreadId`.  Kody's suggested fix, a ref alone, would have left the shortcut loss.  PR 859: `PluginsManagerView` kept hand-written interfaces beside unexported `.passthrough()` schemas and read the response through an `as PluginsResponse` cast; the schemas are exported, the types are `z.infer`, the cast is gone, and unknown fields are stripped, not rejected, so a field the server adds cannot blank the list.  The old interface also made `path` and `url` optional on both source kinds; the derived type makes a folder's path and a remote source's url required.  Tests: `resolveAppContext` cases that fail on the old logic, and `PluginsManagerView.test.ts` for the exported schemas and derived types.
- **2026-10-09 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] iOS parity batch 2b: delete, archive, pin and section bots and rooms; Models screen; automatic update preference (board `4237c65b`, branch `claude/ios-parity-batch-2b`).**  Owner ruling 2026-10-09: bot management and autonomy controls belong on the phone, which supersedes "archived or hidden chat management remains desktop-only" in `docs/ios-companion.md`.  Sidecar (`companion/src/routes.ts`, `proxy.ts`): `PATCH /api/bots/:id` is now allowed but only for `hidden`, `pinned`, `unread`, `chiefOfStaff`, `section` and `pinnedMessageId`, through one shared route constant used by both `ALLOWED` and the body check (`companionBodyCheck`); any other key is a 403 before the harness sees it, value types are checked (the harness stores `pinned`, `hidden` and `unread` as sent), and the validated object is re-serialized rather than piped, so a duplicate key cannot carry a second value past the check.  `DELETE /api/bots/:id` and `DELETE /api/groups/:id`, `POST /api/bots/apply-model-defaults` and `PATCH /api/auto-update` are exact-method additions; `apply-defaults` (computer grants for every bot) now answers a 403 sentence instead of "no route".  Room Pin Message needed nothing: `PATCH /api/groups/:id` was already open.  Harness (`server/index.ts`): new `PATCH /api/auto-update` takes only `enabled` and saves just that key, so the updater's `lastCheckMs` and fingerprint on disk survive; the bot PATCH now rejects non-boolean `pinned`, `hidden` and `unread`.  `electron/updater.mjs` re-reads `autoUpdate.enabled` from the config file on every tick, because it cached the flag in memory and only the desktop's own checkbox updated it, so a phone change would have done nothing until a restart.  Two premises in the brief did not match the source and were handled rather than built: the desktop's Settings > Models stores NO workspace default (its Apply to All Bots block says so; `botDefaults` has no model fields), so there is no narrow read and write route for defaults, only the apply route is opened; and `GET /api/config` already carries `autoUpdate.enabled`, so only the write route is new.  iOS: bot and room long-press menus in the chat list (Pin, Make or Remove Chief Of Staff, Move To Section, Mark As Unread, Archive, Delete), an Archived Bots list with Restore, Delete Bot and Delete Room in the profiles behind a confirmation that names the bot or room and says what is lost (the desktop deletes without asking), Pin Message in the message menu with a pinned banner for bot chats and rooms, a Settings > Models screen (Apply to All Bots with Primary and Fallback 1 to 3, per-bot rows), and an Enable Automatic Update Checks toggle in the Mac Update card with a clear message on a Mac that predates the route.  The phone mirrors the two rules only the desktop menu enforces (keep one active bot; Chief Of Staff needs `capabilities.agentsMcp`, now decoded).  Known gap: iOS `ModelSelection` has no `latest`, so a bot whose model floats ("Latest Sonnet") is pinned by any per-bot edit from the phone, as the profile sheet already did; adding it safely needs the picker to clear it when the model changes.  Verification: `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` clean; `companion/test/routes.test.ts` and the new `companion/test/bot-organize.test.ts` (a stub harness behind the real proxy: every key the bot PATCH reads is a 403 with nothing reaching the harness) 102 passing; seven new `server/index.test.ts` rows for the auto-update route and the organize fields; `swift test` 705 passing; the App target type-checked with `swiftc -typecheck` against the iOS 27 simulator SDK (no xcodebuild, no simulator), and the hosted `Swift tests + iOS build` job is the real compile.  Not verified: a paired phone against a real computer, and the delete flow's navigation timing on iPad.
- **2026-10-09 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Kody sweep, worktree leases: two bot-and-thread pairs can no longer share one worktree, and a branch deletion is refused before the worktree is touched (board `60462e50`, branch `claude/kody-critical-worktree-leases`).**  Two critical Kody findings on merged PR #848, both confirmed on main.  `server/worktree-leases.ts` named the worktree directory `<botId>-<threadId>` after sanitizing, and the sanitizer keeps `-`, so bot `bot-1` on thread `thread-1` and bot `bot-1-thread` on thread `1` shared one directory while holding different lease keys; the second `acquire` then removed and re-created the first turn's live working tree.  The directory is now `<botId>-<16-hex digest of the raw JSON pair>`, and the prune pass compares active paths instead of a rebuilt key.  `release({ removeBranch: true })` always threw AFTER removing the worktree, so no caller could ever delete a branch and the refusal came too late; it is now refused before any removal unless `confirmBranchDeletion` is set, and with it runs `git branch -D`. - **2026-10-08 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Per-bot On/Off switch: nothing new starts for an Off bot, and a running turn finishes (board `ff523a11`, branch `claude/bot-on-off-toggle`).**  Owner ask 2026-10-08: the owner's bots are always busy (Sentry webhooks, routines), which also keeps blocking app updates.  No existing field fit, so `BotRecord.off` is new: `hidden` is an archive that drops the bot from the roster, and the routine manager's `botSnoozes` stop lives in routines.json and is cleared by any person's message, while Off must survive a message and only an explicit Turn On clears it.  Off is refused in `startTurn` ahead of the stop decision (even for a person's own message, code `bot_off`), at the top of the messages route before anything is steered or queued (the app, the phone, the iMessage relay and Linq all land there), before an edit forks the transcript, in `runGroupMemberTurn` plus responder selection (an Off room member is skipped with a notice), in `ask_bot` and `delegate_bot`, and through `botState: "off"` for every automation: a schedule tick, a webhook, a resource trigger and Run now each record a `cancelled` receipt with outcome `bot_off` and the text "Skipped: this bot is off" (not `missed`, so the attention badge stays quiet) and are never retried.  Switching Off settles queued receipts, and for an idle bot drops queued sends and room rounds with a "Not sent" line; a busy bot's are settled when its turn ends, and the running turn is never interrupted.  An Off bot's queued work is left out of update readiness (`turns` still counts a running turn, on purpose: interrupting it would contradict "not interrupted") and out of the roster's engine holds, and job wakes skip it.  UI: On/Off card in Bot Profile (desktop through the existing PATCH and the paired `/profile` route, same auth, `off` added to the shared profile schema), an Off label plus dimmed avatar in the sidebar and the iOS chat list, a disabled composer with Turn On on both clients, and a Power section in the iOS profile.  Verification: typecheck clean; `server/bot-off.test.ts` drives a real harness (restart persistence, the app, iMessage and Linq posts and an edit refused with zero engine requests, a held turn finishing while queued sends are dropped, webhook and Run now skipped, room member skipped); unit, wiring and routine-manager tests; Playwright `bot-off.visual.spec.ts` (rendered on this Mac, committed under the Linux name like the other baselines, so the Ubuntu `E2E smoke` run is the real check); `swift test` 652 passing; unsigned simulator `xcodebuild` succeeded and three simulator screenshots were taken with `-store-preview -preview-off` (the chat list with Pixel dimmed and labelled Off, Pixel's chat with the disabled composer and Turn On, and the Power section of the profile); lint ratchet and `lint-pr-gate` pass.  The full `pnpm test` at host load 100 to 800 failed 21 tests in 7 files (`server/index.test.ts`, `unattended`, `http-lane-e2e`, `decision-log-wiring`, `env-path`, `mcp-server-harness`, `electron/speech-personal-voice`); the webhook ones fail identically at origin/main on this host (webhook runs defer on a hot host), `index.test.ts` failed 48 at origin/main under load 186 versus 15 on this branch at lower load with mostly the same names, and the mcp harness and speech files passed on re-run.  Not verified: a real provider turn, or a paired phone against a real computer.  A phone on this build paired to a Mac without this change will be refused the Power switch (`unsupported profile field: off`) until the Mac updates.

- **2026-10-08 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR (PR #960, not auto-merged) ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Updates never wait on busy bots: hold new work, a 60-second grace, then pause and resume (board `ef645faf`, branch `claude/updater-drain`).**  Owner ruling the same evening: updates must never be blocked by bot work, and interrupting a turn is fine as long as it resumes.  `apply` used to refuse whenever anything ran, and on a webhook-fed Mac that was almost always.  Now every update (CLI, in-app button, phone) asks the harness to hold new work (`POST /api/runtime/quiesce?drain=1`, `server/update-drain.ts`: routine, webhook and resource runs stay queued, job wakes put their notice back, a person's message waits in the steer queue, every route stays open), gives running work 60 seconds (`--grace SECONDS`, `BOTFLEET_UPDATE_GRACE_MS`), then runs the forced quiesce `--force` always used.  Held messages ride `update-held-sends.json`, committed when their bot was idle and carried uncommitted behind a paused bot so its own turn resumes first.  Room work is the one exception: the updater waits up to 5 more minutes for rooms (`BOTFLEET_UPDATE_ROOM_WAIT_MS`) and then refuses cleanly.  `--wait-for-idle [MINUTES]` is the never-interrupt opt-in.  The harness lets go of a hold by itself past the updater's window, and the updater releases on every non-fence exit, signals included.  The preflight now retries a slow harness for up to 60 seconds with 10-second answers, and a forced answer lost to load is watched until the fence settles.  A process that is not BotFleet holding BotFleet state (a bot's own sqlite3 or curl, as at 9:08pm) is re-resolved and waited out for up to 90 seconds (`BOTFLEET_UNKNOWN_HOLDER_WAIT_MS`), never signalled, and named by executable if it stays.  Rollout: `docs/rollouts/2026-10-08-updates-never-wait-on-busy-bots.md`.
- **2026-10-08 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] VM cards tell the truth: no literal \\u00a0, no false desktop-start failures, no "Start docker first" while Docker is up (branch `claude/vm-card-status-truth`).**  Causes: the Shared VPS subtitle was a quoted JSX attribute, which does not process `\u00a0`; both the VPS and Local VM probes pasted the tail of the Driver's stderr log (its update notice, coloured WARN tracing and "daemon listening" line) as "failed to start" and showed a bare timeout beside a running container; and the Local VM header printed the harness's problem string instead of deriving it from `daemonUp`.  New `server/desktop-probe.ts` judges the log, reports a timed-out check as "Couldn't reach the desktop just now; retrying", and strips ANSI for both paths, the Local VM header and Step 4 now name the real next step (prepare the desktop, then create the VM), a source guard bans escapes in quoted JSX attributes, and the `v0.34.0` in the banner is the pinned 0.20.0 Driver announcing the newest release, not the running version (no pins changed).
- **2026-10-07 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] Sentry Crons check-in closes were fire-and-forget, so a saturated host dropped them silently and Sentry later manufactured an error (board `616bcae4`, branch `minimax/sentry-crons-close-flush`, PR #937).**  `checkInRoutineFinish` called `captureCheckIn({status, checkInId})` and returned:  that only enqueues the envelope, so nothing waited for it to leave the process and nothing recorded whether it did.  Under the load recorded with the failure (19-89 per core, swap 91-94%, run `471ead38` at 2026-10-07 22:45Z) the buffered event was dropped, the monitor stayed `in_progress`, and Sentry's cron monitor timed a successful run out as an error ‚Äî a monitoring bug manufacturing an operational alert.  The close is now followed by a detached `sdk.flush(10000)`;  a false result re-sends the same close with the same `checkInId` (idempotent on Sentry's side ‚Äî a duplicate is the same check-in, not a second run) after 5s, 30s and 2m, and three refusals log exactly one line naming the slug and the run, which is the first time this failure mode is visible at all.  Nothing on the run's completion path waits:  the flush is fire-and-forget and the backoff timers are `unref`'d, so a slow or dead Sentry delays neither the finished run nor process shutdown, and the function still never throws.  Tests written against the exact behaviours asked for:  flush refusing twice then accepting sends three closes carrying one `checkInId`;  flush refusing every time sends four closes and logs exactly one line;  inactive Sentry sends nothing at all.  Local: `vitest run server/sentry-crons.test.ts` 15 passed, `pnpm typecheck` clean, `pnpm lint` baseline check passed (one `unicorn/no-useless-spread` regression caught locally before CI).  The full local `pnpm test` never completed ‚Äî this Mac sat at load 538-712 per core ‚Äî so hosted CI is the evidence, reported as such rather than quietly claimed.
- **2026-10-07 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR (PR #945) ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Local VM: the share-CLI-credentials option now carries gh's Keychain token (board `6af489a6`, branch `claude/local-vm-gh-token`).**  macOS gh keeps its token in the Keychain, so the read-only `~/.config/gh` mount had none and the VM's gh was signed out.  With the existing option on, `server/local-vm-gh-credentials.ts` now logs gh in inside the container as cua with the host's `gh auth token` on stdin only (a writable `GH_CONFIG_DIR` and git's github.com helper are set at create), right after create and on each Local VM turn gated by a per-container token hash; the VPS path is untouched and has the same Keychain gap, left as a follow-up after PR #861.
- **2026-10-08 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Updater treats zombie processes as exited (branch `claude/updater-zombie-exit`).**  The apply step rolled back with "BotFleet did not exit after graceful quit and SIGTERM (pid 61872, 89539); refusing SIGKILL" because both pids were already-exited `cua-driver` zombies (state Z, parent a grok CLI that never reaped them) and the `kill -0` liveness probe in `scripts/update-botfleet-mac.mjs` cannot tell a zombie from a running process.  `processIsAlive` now reads `ps -o stat=` and counts a leading Z as exited (failing toward alive when `ps` itself fails), and the capture, quiesce wait, bundle and database holder discovery, `ps` argv match, owner record and update lock all use it, so a zombie is never captured, waited on, or signalled and nothing is ever sent to its parent.
- **2026-10-08 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Hosted Mac updates accept in-bundle framework symlinks, still refuse Zip Slip (branch `claude/hosted-update-framework-symlinks`).**  The safe updater refused the green hosted build of main 8ccf02725 because `scripts/ci-build-resolver.mjs` treated every symlink as an attack, and a real Electron bundle always carries framework links, so the hosted path could never install a real build.  Links strictly inside `BotFleet.app/` with relative, non-climbing targets read from the archive are now accepted, while absolute or `..` targets, links outside the bundle, anything written through a link (case- and normalization-folded), and duplicates are refused before extraction, and a realpath walk of a fresh private extraction directory refuses any link that really lands outside.
- **2026-10-08 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî COMPLETED (PR #947 merged 97caae4d) ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] Pre-merge Mac packaging gate: unsigned arm64 package + prepare-cua + plist handling, always on Renovate PRs (branch `minimax/mac-package-gate`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-minimax-mac-pkg-gate`).**  Owner ask 2026-10-08.  Cause, not missing code: Renovate #919 merged with every REQUIRED check green and broke Mac packaging twice in one bump ‚Äî @trycua/cua-driver 0.20.0 to 0.32.0 had no pinned asset in scripts/prepare-cua.mjs (#943), and the @xmldom/xmldom override reached 0.9.12 so electron-builder plist parsing died inside DOMParser (#946).  Mac Commit Build runs on push to main and is deliberately not a required check, so it was the LAST place either failure could surface, and because ~/apps/update-botfleet.sh installs from the hosted build of main its tip, a post-merge discovery blocks every Mac update (board e851cfc6).  Cost is cheap with evidence: real Mac Commit Build runs finish in 2 to 8 minutes per `gh run list`, and the Renovate build failed in 5.  Gate 1 runs `pnpm build:cua` alone because prepare-cua compares the installed SDK version against its pinned release BEFORE downloading, so a missing pin fails in seconds instead of after package:prepare has spent minutes on tsc and vite.  Gate 2 reuses `package:mac:local` with `CSC_IDENTITY_AUTO_DISCOVERY=false` and `--dir`.  Gate 3 asserts the bundle exists, `plutil -lint`s its Info.plist, and that the build is genuinely UNSIGNED so the gate cannot silently stop exercising the unsigned path.  No certificate, no notarization, no artifact upload ‚Äî Mac Commit Build keeps the artifact and the signature boundary.  Renovate detection is subtler than it looks: PR #919 author was `app/renovate`, NOT `renovate[bot]`, so the workflow passes BOTH `github.actor` and `github.event.pull_request.user.login` plus the head-ref prefix, and a test pins that a human branch like `minimax/renovate-notes` must NOT trip it because a false positive costs a macOS runner.  Reuses ci-change-scope.mjs via a new `isMacPackagingPath` (isPackagingPath plus mac-commit-build.yml and the classifier itself); `isPackagingPath` is deliberately UNCHANGED so the ci.yml Linux package job keeps exactly its current scope, and a test asserts the widening did not narrow it.  Docs-only PRs keep their fast path ‚Äî the classifier answers run=false for them.  Fail-closed on empty changed-path info.  **Not made a required check**, with the reasoning stated in the PR: a path-filtered required check never reports when filtered out and blocks the PR forever, and the only job that could be required is the cheap classifier, which proves nothing about packaging; second, it is a macOS runner, the scarcest class in the fleet.  Renovate PRs always run it, which is where both real failures came from, and Mac Commit Build stays the post-merge backstop.  Branch protection left to the owner.  Verification: `node --test scripts/mac-package-gate.node-test.mjs` 8/8, `node --test scripts/ci-change-scope.node-test.mjs` 7/7, registered as `test:mac-package-gate` in the pnpm test chain.
- **2026-10-08 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Pin @xmldom/xmldom back to 0.8.15 so electron-builder packages again (board `e851cfc6`, same #919 regression, branch `claude/pin-xmldom-0.8`).**  #919 moved the pnpm override for `@xmldom/xmldom` to 0.9.12, and 0.9 requires a mimeType that electron-builder's plist parsing never passes, so Mac Commit Build still failed after #943 with `DOMParser.parseFromString: the provided mimeType "undefined" is not valid`.  This restores the override and only the xmldom lockfile entries to 0.8.15 and adds a Renovate rule capping `@xmldom/xmldom` below 0.9.

- **2026-10-06 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] VM CLI gap list: what else the image layer should carry beyond the 51 (board `6af489a6`, branch `minimax/vm-brew-cli-layer`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-minimax-mcp-registry`).**  Owner asked, while the v6 layer rebuild was in flight, for a list of what else to add and for CLI coverage of the apps he uses as desktop GUI or web.  Diffed the Mac (160 brew formulae, 8 casks, 5 npm globals) against the 51 manifest tools.  **Biggest omission: `ripgrep` is absent entirely** and `grep` is a poor substitute for an agent.  Next tier: `wrangler` and `cloudflared` (the fleet runs Cloudflare heavily and cloudflared is an always-on pm2 job on the Mac), `shellcheck`, `tesseract` plus `poppler` (OCR and pdftotext are daily work on a computer-use desktop), `actionlint`, `gitleaks` (this repo already gates on it in CI), `pm2`, `protoc`, `gdu`, `mosh`, `git-filter-repo`, `mise`, `uv`, `ollama`.  Only three casks have a Linux-viable CLI worth taking: `android-commandlinetools`, `antigravity-cli` (BotFleet already has an antigravity driver), `copilot-cli`.  Named the never-add list so no future pass tries: `xcodegen`, `xcbeautify` (need Xcode), `orbstack` (macOS runtime, VM has Docker), `cleanmymac-cli`, `dockdoor`, `music-decoy`, `mas`, `pinentry-mac`, `mlx` (no GPU), `git-gui`.  **The load-bearing conclusion: for a SaaS or web app there is no CLI worth having, and the right interface is an official MCP server** ‚Äî GitHub via gh plus GitHub MCP, Cloudflare via wrangler plus Cloudflare MCP, Sentry via the sentry-mcp.ts BotFleet already ships, and Linear, Notion, Figma, Slack, Stripe, 1Password as registry rows rather than hand-rolled wrappers.  That makes the manifest the CLI lane and the registry landed in PR #900 the SaaS lane, and it is why the two pieces belong together.  Left open deliberately: whether the VM should carry agent CLIs (claude, codex, cursor-agent, dsh, opencode, antigravity-cli, copilot-cli) for nested agent work ‚Äî unnecessary today because BotFleet spawns drivers on the host, real the moment a bot inside the VM should launch a nested agent, and expensive because each needs its own credentials in the container, so it deserves its own board row.

Confirms the gap: no user-managed MCP registry exists at HEAD, because the `mcpServers` array mounted into a bot turn is assembled in code from BotFleet-owned integrations only (`acpMcpServers`, `server/drivers/acp/core.ts:89`; Claude twin at `server/drivers/claude.ts:1129` before `--strict-mcp-config`), and the 2026-09-11 provider-isolation rollout deliberately keeps the owner global `~/.claude.json` servers invisible.  Cherry Studio parity is still Missing per `docs/audits/2026-09-30-cherry-studio-and-upstream-openmausbot-review.md:96`, with Apache ports `server/mcp-registry.ts` (#697) and `src/components/McpServersPanel.tsx` (#956) identified.  Note the asymmetry: BF is already a strong MCP **server** (`docs/mcp-server.md`, 24 tools) and not yet a client for anything it did not write.  Design lands at `docs/plans/2026-10-06-mcp-plugin-registry.md`: MCP spec transports only (stdio, Streamable HTTP, legacy SSE), import of the ecosystem-standard `mcpServers` JSON stored **disabled** with a command-line preview before first run, env/header values as Infisical `SecretRef`s rather than literals, `--strict-mcp-config` preserved, helpers get no rows, per-row tool allowlist with config-hashed tool ids, lazy connect keyed by config hash, PKCE OAuth with token material in the vault (explicitly not Cherry Studio plain-JSON), cleartext refused off loopback, two trust tiers (owner vs curated reviewed-and-pinned), in-repo catalog with no remote fetch.  Four waves: W1 registry + panel + stdio for engines that take an MCP array, W2 HTTP/SSE + OAuth + allowlist enforcement, W3 a real BotFleet-native MCP client in `buildTurnTools` so the HTTP tool-loop engines get rows too (the real cost centre), W4 curated catalog + drift checks.  Groken (board `2e60c6f6`) is the first candidate row at the **owner** tier, not curated.  AI SDK rejected in the same doc: BF already has the provider layer, catalogs, capability registry, lineage, and a capped fallback walk in `server/model-fallback.ts`, and its strongest engines are CLI/ACP processes rather than API endpoints; the real DeepSeek opportunity is a `deepseekApi` driver on the existing openai-compat shape, with an explicit model allowlist because DeepSeek silently remaps an unrecognized Anthropic-format model name.

- **2026-10-04 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî IN PR (PR #842) ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] Rec 1: the Mac installs a commit-keyed build from GitHub runners instead of compiling on this Mac (board `34c834f1`).**  Honours the owner's 2026-10-01 ruling that Mac runners do the building, always, with a local bypass; `pnpm package:mac:local` is a 10-15 minute `electron-builder` run on the same Mac running five to ten seats, and every recorded update failure was inside it.  New `.github/workflows/mac-commit-build.yml` publishes a signed arm64 `BotFleet.app` keyed by its commit on every push to `main`, with `concurrency` cancelling superseded builds; it is deliberately **not** `release.yml` with a different trigger, because that pipeline notarizes and staples and publishes a version-keyed feed for distribution while this artifact is installed locally.  `buildBundle` tries the hosted build first, defaulting to `BOTFLEET_UPDATE_SOURCE=ci`, and only falls back to a local package for `no-build` or a run the workflow was **expected** to cancel ‚Äî a genuinely failed build is a signal, and quietly building it locally would be a deceptively successful install.  `scripts/ci-build-resolver.mjs` accepts only a successful run for this exact SHA, ignores expired artifacts, verifies the manifest's commit and sha256, and then hands the unpacked `.app` to the existing `validateBuiltBundle`, which still demands the exact Developer ID team and designated requirement.  **The signature is the security boundary; the manifest only guards the transfer** ‚Äî a manifest naming a different commit is refused even though its bytes would pass a signature check.  **On the Option A vs C question filed Oct 2: neither is needed**, because CODEX's finding holds ‚Äî `dependencyFingerprint` hashes only files present in the staging tree and never anything inside the bundle, so a CI-sourced `.app` paired with locally installed `node_modules` does not weaken the invariant and the 2.0 GiB tree is never transferred.  **Also fixed a latent footgun:** `update-botfleet.sh` bootstraps the updater by archiving a literal list of files out of the commit it is installing, and nothing in the JS noticed a new import ‚Äî so adding one would break `ubf` on every Mac *at the moment it is trying to recover from a failed update*.  The only warning was a comment; `scripts/update-botfleet-arch.node-test.mjs` now enforces the list in both directions and is mutation-checked.  **Two production bugs were caught only after correcting the test fixture to reproduce the real two-level artifact shape GitHub produces:** the wrapper nests entries under a directory named for the artifact, and `ditto -x -k` extracts *into* its destination.  An idealized fixture hid both.

- **2026-10-04 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî IN PR, BOTH BLOCKED ONLY ON REVIEW ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] Four updater recommendations: three landed, one scoped, and a fleet-wide ledger incident fixed (boards `34c834f1`, `49465fcb`, `1f3fe835`, `f1482275`, incident `a90e1f15`).**  Jay approved all four MCode-style background-updater recommendations.  **Rec 4** (PR #842) adds a `smokeTestBundle` step between `validateBundle` and `persistPrepared` ‚Äî the last point that still touches nothing live ‚Äî which boots the candidate on the packaged Electron binary under `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, requires a validated health body and an owner record naming the probe's own pid, and round-trips a row through `node:sqlite` on `:memory:`.  Verified against the real installed signed bundle, not only by unit test: `ready`, `sqliteOk`, 17.6s quiet and 51.4s while four other seats were compiling.  **Rec 1** (PR #842) adds a commit-keyed signed arm64 build on every push to main and makes `ubf` download it, defaulting to `ci`, falling back to a local package only for "never built" or "cancelled as superseded".  **Rec 3** is committed and verified.  **Rec 2** is scoped to the HARNESS ONLY (PR #858): immutable `releases/<commit>` behind an atomically swapped pointer, plus the launcher changes that make a read-only release runnable.  The app half ‚Äî retiring Squirrel so the app path can become a symlink ‚Äî is deliberately NOT done, because it is guarded by the critical Kody rule `do-not-change-sparkle-feed-url`, and that is the owner's call rather than an agent's.  **Rec 2 is not activated on this Mac**: the live `app.botfleet.server` still runs from its checkout, and moving the LaunchAgent `WorkingDirectory` pauses for Jay.  **Both PRs are fully green on CI and blocked only by a standing Kody `CHANGES_REQUESTED`** that a commented review does not dismiss; dismissing it or approving unblocks them, and I did not force-merge over it.  **Fleet incident:** `docs/EFFORT-LOG.md` was truncated from 716 rows to 9 on main ‚Äî every seat's history at once ‚Äî by `open(path, "w").write(row + open(path).read())`, which truncates before it reads.  Restored to 725 rows in PR #846, with a guard that fails any diff removing more rows than it adds; PR #850 added the `merge=union` attribute the fleet's own union script already assumed, and #842's next rebase passed with ZERO conflicts, the first clean one this branch has had. - **2026-10-04 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî COMMITTED, AWAITING MERGE ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] Rec 3: `--components server|app|both` decouples a server update from the desktop relaunch.** The coupling was never one step: it was spread across six, each individually reasonable ‚Äî `quiesce` alone booted out the harness AND sent AppleScript quit to the app AND swept the app's pids. A server-only run now stops the harness, swaps the dependency tree, restarts it, and proves it is the single authenticated data owner, without quitting the app, renaming its bundle, preparing credentials, reopening it, or sampling its processes. Verified rather than assumed the fact that makes it safe: `electron/main.mjs:241-247` documents attach-or-spawn, so the app is a **client** of the harness ‚Äî against the always-on `app.botfleet.server` it is attached and that harness is never killed on quit, so a server-only update is clean. The usage text carries the honest caveat that on a desktop with no always-on harness, where the app spawned its own, that child lives until the app restarts. `swapDependencyTree` is the dependency half of `swapPreparedFiles` with identical progress flags and restore-on-failure behaviour, because rollback and the receipt handling depend on those flags. `--components app` is refused without a staged import because the transaction's safety proof is the harness verifying itself as the single authenticated data owner. The parsed CLI is the single source of truth for the selection rather than a second threaded value, which is a way for the bundle swap and the relaunch to disagree. Verification: `pnpm test:mac-updater` 147 pass, `server/update-control` 63 pass, `pnpm typecheck` clean.

- **2026-10-04 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî IN PR (PR #842, auto-merge armed) ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] Addressed the Kody review of the updater-architecture branch: 14 threads, 11 defects found in my own change (board `34c834f1`, `49465fcb`; branch `minimax/updater-architecture`).**  The PR was not stuck on CI ‚Äî all 11 checks were green and it sat `BLOCKED` because Kody had posted `CHANGES_REQUESTED` with 14 unresolved threads.  **Coordination (the preflight this row previously omitted):** read the fleet `AGENT-SYNC.md` protocol before the first message, loaded the `session-start` skill, filed and claimed boards `34c834f1`, `f1482275`, `1f3fe835` and `49465fcb`, and posted `[MINIMAX] sync-1` to `#agent-sync` with `repo: BotFleet` first, before any file was edited; `sync-2` and `sync-3` followed at each completed unit.  **The finding that would have shipped a broken gate: the smoke probe was testing the wrong runtime.**  It booted the candidate with the updater's own Node, but the harness launches the packaged Electron binary under `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` (`server/index.ts`'s `AGENTS_NODE_FLAG`), which is why `electron-builder.yml` keeps the `runAsNode` fuse on ‚Äî so the Homebrew or nvm Node could have `node:sqlite` while Electron's bundled Node did not, or the reverse, and the gate could fail a healthy build or pass a broken one.  It now runs `Contents/MacOS/BotFleet` with the flag, and the SQLite probe uses that same binary so both halves of the gate agree on what is under test.  Re-verified against the real installed signed bundle `d9e646ffc292`: `ready`, `sqliteOk`, **17.6s on a quiet Mac and 51.4s while four other seats were compiling** ‚Äî which is exactly why the boot budget is 180s and not tighter.  **A failed build was being reported as a GitHub outage:** `selectCommitRun` accepts only `conclusion === "success"`, so a run that existed but failed, was cancelled, or was still running fell through to `github-unavailable` with a fabricated `status: 200`; the operator was told to retry something retrying cannot fix, and because the cause was not `no-build`, `isRecoverableResolutionFailure` also blocked the `auto` fallback that exists for exactly this case.  Since this workflow deliberately cancels superseded builds that is the *expected* path, so it now has its own `build-failed` cause, a message naming the conclusion, and its place in the `auto` fallback.  **An untrusted body could establish readiness**, because `body?.ready !== false` accepts a truncated body, an HTML error page, and a bare `{}` ‚Äî every one of them is "not false"; `parseHealthBody` now requires an object with a boolean `ready` and a string `app`.  Also: GitHub API responses are shape-checked before any field is read (`assertWorkflowRun` / `assertArtifact`, malformed entries skipped rather than trusted); raw child stderr is drained and discarded instead of reaching loggable errors; the download has one 12-minute budget rather than three 10-minute ceilings that could hold the updater lock ~30 minutes, about twice the local build it replaced, on the path that now runs by default; and the hard-coded loopback Sentry DSN is gone, because the child environment is built from scratch rather than inherited, so omitting the variable is what actually guarantees the probe cannot reach a real project.  **Two zod suggestions are answered rather than implemented, and the reason is structural, not preference:** the updater bootstraps by archiving a five-file graph into a `mktemp` directory with no `node_modules` beside it, and every module in that graph imports nothing but `node:` builtins, so a bare `import { z } from "zod"` would resolve in the repo and throw `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` on every Mac *at the moment it is trying to recover from a failed update*.  The rule's intent is met by explicit shape checks with the reason recorded beside them, and the reply on each thread argues the point.  **Coordination catch: Jay had already pushed `06834c949` fixing five of these on the same branch**, so rather than duplicate or conflict I rebased onto his commit and re-applied only the seven he had not done, leaving the overlap as his version.  Unresolved threads went 14 to 3, the three being the answered ones awaiting the reviewer.  Verification: `ci-build-resolver` 13 pass (new cases for traversal names, malformed API entries, expired artifacts, the build-failed classification), `update-progress` + `update-control` 78 pass, `pnpm test:mac-updater` 144 pass, `pnpm typecheck` clean.  **Two production bugs were caught only after correcting the test fixture to reproduce the real two-level artifact shape GitHub produces:** the wrapper nests entries under a directory named for the artifact, and `ditto -x -k` extracts *into* its destination ‚Äî an idealized fixture hid both.  **The same pass found a THIRD load-bearing invariant duplicated across two files with a comment claiming parity** ‚Äî `KNOWN_STAGE_ENTRIES`, whose only decider is the server's copy, so every stage the default `ci` policy produced was kept forever with a full extra copy of the app and a multi-gigabyte dependency tree inside it.  **Self-inflicted defect worth recording:** the effort-log writes for this lane used `open(p,'w').write(row + open(p).read())`, which truncates before the read, so two commits deleted other seats' rows from the branch's ledger.  Caught by counting rows per commit rather than trusting the write; main was never affected, and the branch's ledger is now main's file with this lane's rows prepended.

Eliminates breathless run-on speech in MiniMax TTS (t2a_v2) by sanitizing em-dashes, en-dashes, and isolated hyphens into natural breathing pauses and periods across `server/tts/minimax.ts` and `server/tts/speech-summary.ts`. - **2026-10-01 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî COMPLETED (PR #774, merged `f6c49084`) ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] `ubf` bootstrap silently no-ops when TMPDIR is under `/var` (branch `claude/ubf-bootstrap-realpath`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-claude-ubf-bootstrap`).**  The wrapper runs the updater from a `mktemp -d` under `/var/folders`, Node realpaths the ESM entry to `/private/var/...`, and the `import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(process.argv[1])` guard in `scripts/update-botfleet-mac.mjs` is false, so `main()` never runs and the wrapper exits 0 with no output.  Fix: realpath-safe entry guards (updater plus the other scripts with the same pattern), `pwd -P` on the bootstrap dir, a symlinked-path regression test, and an honest `--force` description (it also interrupts busy bots via a forced quiesce).
- **2026-10-01 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR (PR #771) ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Stop tracking `scripts/__pycache__/sentry-ci-report.cpython-314.pyc` (branch `claude/untrack-pycache`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-claude-pycache`).**  PR #689 committed the bytecode cache even though `.gitignore` already ignores `__pycache__/` and `*.pyc`.  `pnpm test:ci-scope` imports `scripts/sentry-ci-report.py` through python3 and rewrites it, so every worktree was dirty after `pnpm test`.  Removing it from the index lets the existing ignore rules apply; no source changes.

- **2026-09-30 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî COMPLETED (BotFleet PR #759; config fix in ~/.dsh/settings.yaml) ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] dsh offered MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview that the bridge always rejected (branch `minimax/dsh-model-and-cli-fixes`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-mm-mcode`).**  Owner ask: test mcode in the Harness app and BotFleet's use of Harness, then address every issue found.  Harness app is alive (`:3080` answers 401 in 0.06s on a tokenised request; a bare `GET /` hangs, which is what made it look dead).  The MMH MiniMax path works (`dsh --profile mmh-headless` returns PONG), and BotFleet's `dshAgent` driver reaches the live bridge and answers on its default model in 12.7s.  **The real bug:** a turn on `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview` failed every time with `Invalid params: unknown model option`.  My first instinct ‚Äî drop the row via `DSH_EXCLUDED_MODEL_IDS` ‚Äî was **wrong**, and tracing the provenance is what proved it.  That id is a hardcoded row in BotFleet's own `STATIC_DSH_MODELS`, and its comment states it is the canonical DSH-hosted MiniMax row per an explicit product decision, so excluding it would have silently undone that decision.  The actual cause was runtime config: `~/.dsh/settings.yaml` declared the minimax provider with only M2.7, M2.7-highspeed and M3, and `subagent-model-selection.allowedModels` likewise omitted it, so the bridge really did not offer it.  Fixed in `settings.yaml` (backup left alongside as `settings.yaml.bak.minimax-m31.*`): the model added to `providers.minimax.models[]` at contextWindow 1000000 / maxTokens 512000, and to `subagent-model-selection.allowedModels`.  Verified end to end ‚Äî the same turn now answers `M31-OK` in 22.9s.  **mcode launcher:** `~/.local/bin/mcode` is a symlink to a launcher that resolves its own data dir from the parent of its own path, so through the symlink it looks for `~/.local/current` and exits non-zero; the canonical `~/.minimax-code/bin` copy is the one that runs.  PR #682 had added the canonical dir to `knownDirs()` but placed it after `~/.local/bin`, so it changed nothing ‚Äî and since a GUI-launched BotFleet inherits a PATH with no mcode in it, `knownDirs` order is the only thing that decides the candidate.  PR #759 puts canonical installer dirs first, and `findCliCandidates("mcode")` under a bare GUI PATH now returns only the working launcher.  **Seat worktrees missing `node_modules/harness`** was transient, not a product bug: a full `pnpm install` completes in about 2m18s and restores it, after which `dshAgent` imports from a lane again.  **Caught before it shipped:** my first commit on #759 swallowed 34 unrelated file deletions left by a stale worktree checkout.  `git reset --hard origin/main` restored them, the branch was force-pushed, and the final commit is 2 files at +25/-2.  - **2026-09-30 ‚Äî AG ‚Äî COMPLETED (PR #755 merged `68658ba30`, deployed) ‚Äî [BF][AG] Chained DeepSeek V4.1 Flash TTS voice summary post-processor, bracket tag stripping, per-bot voice mode resolution, real-time word-level bold highlighting, and settings UX polish (branch `ag/chained-tts-voice-summary`, worktree `~/Code/BotFleet`).**  Adds chained DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash distillation layer for MiniMax TTS speech synthesis with prompt optimization and `thinking: { type: "disabled" }` for sub-800ms speed.  Strips bracketed voice summary tags from UI bubbles so normal written messages render smoothly.  Adds per-bot 3-way mode (`on_demand`, `always`, `off`) with automatic default resolution (`always` for voice-enabled bots, `on_demand` for text-only bots), deduplicated generation jobs, secret redaction, and bounded 10-second timeout.  Adds real-time word-by-word bold highlighting in ChatView SpokenSummaryCard synchronized with audio playback progress, dedicated `DEEPSEEK_VOICE_API_KEY` credential support, and external benchmark findings documentation link in VoiceSettings.  All 11 CI checks green, PR #755 merged to main, web dist rebuilt in `~/apps/botfleet-server`, and local harness verified healthy on port 8799.

- **2026-09-28 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî COMPLETED (PR #720 merged) ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] Per-trigger error badges on the Webhooks and Resources lists (branch `minimax/trigger-error-badges`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-mm-trigger-badges`).**  Follow-up to PR #705: the header badge says how much is broken fleet-wide but not which trigger, so one webhook failing every minute and four hundred stale failures from last month look identical in the total.  Adds `TriggerErrorBadge` to both trigger lists, showing that trigger's own unseen failure count with the last-hour and last-24-hour split in its tooltip; one click acknowledges that trigger's backlog, keeping status, error, and history, and the next failure raises it again.  Grouping is a pure function (`summarizeTriggerAttention`) keyed on `triggerSource` + `webhookId`, since a webhook run and a resource firing both carry the trigger id in `webhookId` ‚Äî and the acknowledge sweep sends the source too, so clearing one badge can never clear the other's failures, which is what a `webhookId`-only filter would have done.  The badge uses the same one-minute `useNow` tick as the page summary so the windows cannot freeze while the panel sits open.  Webhooks sidebar rows restructured from a bare button to a wrapper with the select button and the badge as siblings, because a button inside a button is not a button. - **2026-10-04 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî IN PR (PR #842, auto-merge armed) ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] Addressed the Kody review of the updater-architecture branch: 14 threads, 11 defects found in my own change (board `34c834f1`, `49465fcb`; branch `minimax/updater-architecture`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-minimax-updater-arch`).**  The PR was not stuck on CI ‚Äî all 11 checks were green and it sat `BLOCKED` because Kody had posted `CHANGES_REQUESTED` with 14 unresolved threads.  **Coordination (the preflight this row previously omitted):** read `/Users/jay/apps/AGENT-SYNC.md` before the first message, loaded the `session-start` skill, loaded and claimed boards `34c834f1`, `f1482275`, `1f3fe835` and `49465fcb`, and posted `[MINIMAX] sync-1` to `#agent-sync` (channel `C0BEZDJDNKV`) with `repo: BotFleet` first, before any file was edited; `sync-2` and `sync-3` followed at each completed unit.  **The finding that would have shipped a broken gate: the smoke probe was testing the wrong runtime.**  It booted the candidate with the updater's own Node, but the harness launches the packaged Electron binary under `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` (`server/index.ts`'s `AGENTS_NODE_FLAG`), which is why `electron-builder.yml` keeps the `runAsNode` fuse on ‚Äî so the Homebrew or nvm Node could have `node:sqlite` while Electron's bundled Node did not, or the reverse, and the gate could fail a healthy build or pass a broken one.  It now runs `Contents/MacOS/BotFleet` with the flag, and the SQLite probe uses that same binary so both halves of the gate agree on what is under test.  Re-verified against the real installed signed bundle `d9e646ffc292`: `ready`, `sqliteOk`, **17.6s on a quiet Mac and 51.4s while four other seats were compiling** ‚Äî which is exactly why the boot budget is 180s and not tighter.  **A failed build was being reported as a GitHub outage:** `selectCommitRun` accepts only `conclusion === "success"`, so a run that existed but failed, was cancelled, or was still running fell through to `github-unavailable` with a fabricated `status: 200`; the operator was told to retry something retrying cannot fix, and because the cause was not `no-build`, `isRecoverableResolutionFailure` also blocked the `auto` fallback that exists for exactly this case.  Since this workflow deliberately cancels superseded builds that is the *expected* path, so it now has its own `build-failed` cause, a message naming the conclusion, and its place in the `auto` fallback.  **An untrusted body could establish readiness**, because `body?.ready !== false` accepts a truncated body, an HTML error page, and a bare `{}` ‚Äî every one of them is "not false"; `parseHealthBody` now requires an object with a boolean `ready` and a string `app`.  Also: GitHub API responses are shape-checked before any field is read (`assertWorkflowRun` / `assertArtifact`, malformed entries skipped rather than trusted); raw child stderr is drained and discarded instead of reaching loggable errors; the download has one 12-minute budget rather than three 10-minute ceilings that could hold the updater lock ~30 minutes, about twice the local build it replaced, on the path that now runs by default; and the hard-coded loopback Sentry DSN is gone, because the child environment is built from scratch rather than inherited, so omitting the variable is what actually guarantees the probe cannot reach a real project.  **Two zod suggestions are answered rather than implemented, and the reason is structural, not preference:** the updater bootstraps by archiving a five-file graph into a `mktemp` directory with no `node_modules` beside it, and every module in that graph imports nothing but `node:` builtins, so a bare `import { z } from "zod"` would resolve in the repo and throw `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` on every Mac *at the moment it is trying to recover from a failed update*.  The rule's intent is met by explicit shape checks with the reason recorded beside them, and the reply on each thread argues the point.  **Coordination catch: Jay had already pushed `06834c949` fixing five of these on the same branch**, so rather than duplicate or conflict I rebased onto his commit and re-applied only the seven he had not done, leaving the overlap as his version.  Unresolved threads went 14 to 3, the three being the answered ones awaiting the reviewer.  Verification: `ci-build-resolver` 13 pass (new cases for traversal names, malformed API entries, expired artifacts, the build-failed classification), `update-progress` + `update-control` 78 pass, `pnpm test:mac-updater` 144 pass, `pnpm typecheck` clean.

- **2026-10-09 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî IN PR (PR #1021) ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Fleet Matrix overview stays open until you pick a chat: streamed bot and room updates and the "All" tab no longer close it (board `6689e3f3`, branch `claude/overview-dismiss`, lane `~/apps/lanes/botfleet/claude-overview-dismiss`).**  Cause: the `src/App.tsx` effect that yields the overview to a selection watched `state.bots`, `state.groups`, `viewedThreadId` and `selectedAppId` (#854 widened it, because its `selectedAppId` cleanup needs them), and `selectedId` is set for the whole session, so any SSE update, or "All" with a room open, shut the overview at once.  Fix: a `selectionNonce` in the store, moved by `select` (a click on the chat already open included, which `selectedId` cannot show) and by `botAdded`; `useDismissOnSelection` closes the overview only when it moves; the #854 cleanup stays as its own effect.  Tests: reducer, hook, and `tests/e2e/fleet-matrix-overview.spec.ts` (two of its four fail on the old `App.tsx`).

Commit 3bbaf31f9 pushed to minimax/computer-preview-fallback, rebased onto a concurrent writer's 570af65da, no force. The briefing's two named findings (:526 comparator, :553 inFlight) were ALREADY RESOLVED in a prior round - those line numbers are stale against HEAD; both were verified against source rather than the diffHunk (newestPreview at src/lib/computer-preview.ts:126-134 compares by age with a documented tie-break and correct 0-timestamp handling; the inFlight component state no longer exists, it is now pendingCapture/panelLive refs with a 6-case test at computer-preview.test.ts:127-160). The one real fix: captureProblem was cleared only at mount or by the next good capture, so a resumed stream stranded the banner over a healthy preview.
