- **2026-09-27 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî IN PR (PR #693, auto-merge armed) ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] Bot settings: the round budget is one number, and the tool-rounds control stops vanishing (branch `minimax/bot-settings-gates`).**  Lane at `~/apps/botfleet-mm-bot-settings`.  Owner report: ‚Äúonly a couple bots have setting for how many tool rounds shown.‚Äù  Two independent defects.  **(1) The round budget was three literals that disagreed** ‚Äî `toolBudgetPrompt` told the turn it had 40 rounds (`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS`), `DEFAULT_TURN_LOOP_BUDGET.maxRounds` hard-stopped it at 12, and the desktop plus iOS copy said ‚ÄúEmpty uses 12‚Äù.  An unset bot was promised 40, spend against 40, and was cut off at 12 mid-work ‚Äî exactly the truncation `toolBudgetPrompt` exists to prevent.  The test that should have caught it asserted the literal string ‚ÄúEmpty uses 12‚Äù was PRESENT in two files, which passes just as happily on a wrong number as a right one.  Fixed structurally: the hard stop derives from the shared constant, the caption is generated by `toolRoundsCaption()`, and `loop.test.ts` pins the agreement between the number promised and the number enforced.  Unset default therefore moves 12 ‚Üí 40 (owner-visible change; one constant to revert).  **(2) The control vanished whenever the engine lookup had not answered** ‚Äî the gate was `engine?.capabilities?.toolLoop === true`, where `undefined` reads as ‚Äúno‚Äù, so it disappeared while `/api/instances` was in flight and for any engine the client had never heard of; only minimax, grok and openai-compat declare `toolLoop` at all.  Nothing distinguished ‚Äúengine ignores this‚Äù from ‚Äúyou never set one‚Äù, so a ceiling saved on MiniMax stayed on the record invisible, inert and un-clearable.  New `src/lib/bot-settings-gates.ts` is the single decision point: a saved value stays visible read-only with the reason, an unknown engine is never told ‚Äúno‚Äù, a fallback rollover names the engine actually running, and every capability row reads `botCapabilityGates()` once.  iOS gets the same rule.  **Testing** ‚Äî the string-presence assertions are replaced by real behaviour: gate unit tests, `renderToStaticMarkup` render tests (the repo‚Äôs DOM-free pattern), and a server test that the number promised equals the number enforced.  **Verification** ‚Äî `pnpm typecheck` clean; 135 tests across the 6 affected files; `cd ios && swift test` 446 tests 0 failures; CI `lint` green and the diff adds zero violations (net ‚àí3) against a clean `origin/main` worktree.  **Also found and filed** ‚Äî : the per-rule lint baseline is 81 warnings stale versus main (4100 vs 4019), so the main-push lint job is red.  First filed P0 on the wrong premise that it blocked PRs; corrected in a board comment after reading `node-ci.yml` ‚Äî PRs run `lint-pr-gate.mjs` (errors in touched files only) and the full ratchet is a non-PR gate.

(4) The updater contention in the handoff was self-inflicted: pid 42253 was CLAUDE's own run, and the 2:13 AM refusal at the lock was a non-interactive agent call, so no shell history names it.  A prior MINIMAX session already owns the live update under launchd label `com.jay.botfleet-update`, so no second run was started.  Local VM verification is partial and honest: the VM is still off (`botDefaults.computerProviders.localVm = false`), and the 3 bots that started after the relaunch are working with zero "Create the Local VM" errors, but the other 4 of the 7 have not run yet, so they are unverified. - **2026-10-02 ‚Äî MINIMAX (picked up from CLAUDE, PR #800 open) ‚Äî IN PROGRESS ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] Quarantine corrupt bots.json, groups.json and routines.json, and salvage the valid sections of config.json (board `230ef988`, audit rows A7 and C3, branch `claude/store-quarantine`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-claude-store-quarantine`).**  A parse failure today yields an empty roster that the next save makes permanent, a schema-invalid config.json field runs the whole session on defaults with no log line, and unparseable JSON is replaced by the next patch write.  This lane renames an unreadable store to `<name>.corrupt-<epoch>` (never deleted), refuses to save over it, shows a banner, and makes loadConfig warn once and keep every section that still validates.  Claimed in #agent-sync (`C0BEZDJDNKV`) as `[CLAUDE] repo: BotFleet ‚Äî quarantine corrupt persisted stores` on Fri, Oct 2, 2026, 3:15 AM CT, and taken over by MINIMAX as `[MINIMAX] repo: BotFleet ‚Äî quarantine corrupt persisted stores` when that session parked on quota.  An independent review found the branch's central durability claim untrue in its main path: the set-aside copy was a bare `copyFileSync` with no fsync, so the only record of the entries a partial load could not parse was not on stable storage, while the pruned file written over the original by `writeFileAtomic` was ‚Äî so a power loss between the two took those entries for good.  It now flushes the copy before reporting success and fails closed if it cannot.  The same review found that a process dying between the config lock's two renames leaves no config.json with its contents set aside, and `readStoredConfig` read that ENOENT as an ordinary first run: defaults, no fault, no log, no banner, while `registerLeftOverSetAsideFiles` deliberately did not raise config.json, so nothing else surfaced it.  A missing config is now a first run only when nothing of it is left over, and a quarantined config.json gets a left-over notice of its own, since the copy left behind is the one holding the owner's API keys and the file that replaced it is healthy.  Retention of `.corrupt-` files is filed as board `a2614435` and deliberately not auto-expired, because a set-aside file is the only copy of what could not be parsed and expiring it would trade a visible recoverable state for silent data loss.  PR #800 open, auto-merge not yet armed.  Ten Kody review findings addressed in `7cabf22f6`: a refused write no longer clears its pending flag in either the routine manager or the store, `deleteGroup` and `deleteBot` stop before deleting message rows, transcripts and a workspace when the roster cannot be written, a quarantined config.json now raises a left-over notice, the set-aside probe is answered from a listing cached against the data directory's own mtime, ctime and size rather than a `readdirSync(DATA_DIR)` on every `loadConfig()`, three tests that asked the platform whether a refusal could be set up now assert the outcome it produced, and the fabricated `sk-` credential fixtures are now `REDACTED_TEST_MARKER`.

- **2026-10-01 ‚Äî CLAUDE ‚Äî COMPLETED (PR #792 merged `89e994a9e`, review follow-up PR #794) ‚Äî [BF][CLAUDE] Composer keeps the draft until a send is accepted and restores it when the send is rejected or the server is unreachable (board `cfb03f4e`, id `cherry-chat-ux-1`, branch `claude/composer-draft-restore`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-claude-composer-draft-restore`).**  Before this the composer cleared text, attachment chips and the reply target right after a fire-and-forget POST, so a rejected send lost a long prompt with no ghost message.  The composer still clears at once, which is what stops a second Enter from sending the same text twice.  The send and sendGroup actions take an optional `onError` that runs after the error banner when the server refuses the send or cannot be reached, and the composer uses it to put the text, the attachment chips and the reply target back.  A restore goes to the open composer when there is one and to the stored draft otherwise, keeps anything typed since, and never doubles a chip.  #794 adds the review follow-ups: a second room message held for a busy member joins the first instead of replacing it, and the verification recipe and comments match what was built.  Follow-ups: send an idempotency key so a retried send cannot duplicate a message the server did save, and surface a self-echo answered with 200 instead of dropping it.  Kept the Composer.tsx edit small because the slash menu lane edits the same file after this one.

- **2026-09-30 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî COMPLETED (BotFleet PR #759; config fix in ~/.dsh/settings.yaml) ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] dsh offered MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview that the bridge always rejected (board `2a7e79ab`, branch `minimax/dsh-model-and-cli-fixes`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-mm-mcode`).**  Owner ask: test mcode in the Harness app and BotFleet's use of Harness, then address every issue found.  Harness app is alive (`:3080` answers 401 in 0.06s on a tokenised request; a bare `GET /` hangs, which is what made it look dead).  The MMH MiniMax path works (`dsh --profile mmh-headless` returns PONG), and BotFleet's `dshAgent` driver reaches the live bridge and answers on its default model in 12.7s.  **The real bug:** a turn on `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview` failed every time with `Invalid params: unknown model option`.  My first instinct ‚Äî drop the row via `DSH_EXCLUDED_MODEL_IDS` ‚Äî was **wrong**, and tracing the provenance is what proved it.  That id is a hardcoded row in BotFleet's own `STATIC_DSH_MODELS`, and its comment states it is the canonical DSH-hosted MiniMax row per an explicit product decision, so excluding it would have silently undone that decision.  The actual cause was runtime config: `~/.dsh/settings.yaml` declared the minimax provider with only M2.7, M2.7-highspeed and M3, and `subagent-model-selection.allowedModels` likewise omitted it, so the bridge really did not offer it.  Fixed in `settings.yaml` (backup left alongside as `settings.yaml.bak.minimax-m31.*`): the model added to `providers.minimax.models[]` at contextWindow 1000000 / maxTokens 512000, and to `subagent-model-selection.allowedModels`.  Verified end to end ‚Äî the same turn now answers `M31-OK` in 22.9s.  **mcode launcher:** `~/.local/bin/mcode` is a symlink to a launcher that resolves its own data dir from the parent of its own path, so through the symlink it looks for `~/.local/current` and exits non-zero; the canonical `~/.minimax-code/bin` copy is the one that runs.  PR #682 had added the canonical dir to `knownDirs()` but placed it after `~/.local/bin`, so it changed nothing ‚Äî and since a GUI-launched BotFleet inherits a PATH with no mcode in it, `knownDirs` order is the only thing that decides the candidate.  PR #759 puts canonical installer dirs first, and `findCliCandidates("mcode")` under a bare GUI PATH now returns only the working launcher.  **Seat worktrees missing `node_modules/harness`** was transient, not a product bug: a full `pnpm install` completes in about 2m18s and restores it, after which `dshAgent` imports from a lane again.  **Caught before it shipped:** my first commit on #759 swallowed 34 unrelated file deletions left by a stale worktree checkout.  `git reset --hard origin/main` restored them, the branch was force-pushed, and the final commit is 2 files at +25/-2.  - **2026-09-30 ‚Äî AG ‚Äî COMPLETED (PR #755 merged `68658ba30`, deployed) ‚Äî [BF][AG] Chained DeepSeek V4.1 Flash TTS voice summary post-processor, bracket tag stripping, per-bot voice mode resolution, real-time word-level bold highlighting, and settings UX polish (branch `ag/chained-tts-voice-summary`, worktree `~/Code/BotFleet`).**  Adds chained DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash distillation layer for MiniMax TTS speech synthesis with prompt optimization and `thinking: { type: "disabled" }` for sub-800ms speed.  Strips bracketed voice summary tags from UI bubbles so normal written messages render smoothly.  Adds per-bot 3-way mode (`on_demand`, `always`, `off`) with automatic default resolution (`always` for voice-enabled bots, `on_demand` for text-only bots), deduplicated generation jobs, secret redaction, and bounded 10-second timeout.  Adds real-time word-by-word bold highlighting in ChatView SpokenSummaryCard synchronized with audio playback progress, dedicated `DEEPSEEK_VOICE_API_KEY` credential support, and external benchmark findings documentation link in VoiceSettings.  All 11 CI checks green, PR #755 merged to main, web dist rebuilt in `~/apps/botfleet-server`, and local harness verified healthy on port 8799.

- **2026-09-27 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî IN PR (PR #691, auto-merge armed) ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] Redesign the Engines settings capability matrix: whole table visible, readable cell detail, no hidden engines (branch `minimax/engines-matrix-redesign`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-mm-engines-matrix`).**  Owner ask, verbatim: "thoroughly redesign and improve the bot engines settings capability matrix and capability definitions, since the way it is now you can't see the whole table ever, and mousing over certain columns shows you text below that can't even mostly see, so it is just not working as intended ‚Äî and also needed further improvement even if it was working as intended."  Four real defects, all confirmed against source before implementation.  (1) The table could NEVER fit: the dialog caps at 1100px, the `shrink-0` nav eats 190px, the pane adds a 40px gutter, so the content pane offers 870px, while the table declared 160px + (8 x 120px) = 1120px; the first column was `sticky left-0`, so roughly 5.9 of 8 engines were ever on screen and the rest was a horizontal scroll nobody found.  mcode (PR #682) made it worse by adding an eighth engine column.  (2) The hover explanation rendered AFTER the table at the bottom of the card ‚Äî a 12-row table ends ~570-600px into a ~650px viewport, so the block began at or past the fold, and `onMouseLeave` cleared the state, so scrolling toward it destroyed it.  That is exactly the "text below that can't even mostly see".  (3) Engines that are not installed were not rendered AT ALL: `showDisabled` defaulted to `false`, so every instance failing `isEngineEnabled` sat behind a `Show (N)` count.  A content bug, not a layout bug, and it survived every resize.  (4) Missing registry keys masqueraded as "no": `capabilities` is `Partial<Record<...>>` and the matrix resolved a missing key through `CELL_TONE[state ?? ""] ?? CELL_TONE.no` while `capabilityCellLabel(undefined)` returned the `‚Äî` glyph, so an unaudited pair rendered as a dash wearing the not-available colour.  The source comments show this class has bitten at least four separate times.  Fix: the matrix is TRANSPOSED (engines are rows, capabilities are columns, grouped into four named categories), which both fits and lines up with the engine rail directly below it.  The detail strip now LATCHES ‚Äî always rendered at a fixed `min-height`, takes the last hovered OR focused cell, and never clears on `mouseleave`, so hovering cannot shift the layout under the pointer and focus latches too for keyboard use.  `unknown` became a real verdict with its own dashed tone, and 13 unaudited pairs are flagged honestly rather than asserted either way.  `CAPABILITY_NOTES` plus a per-engine override now explain what each capability actually IS in BotFleet, so the strip teaches instead of re-reading the engine's pitch.  `showDisabled` defaults to `true` and each row reports the real availability reason from fields the runtime actually provides.  Dialog cap 720px -> 880px, nav 190px -> 164px.  The no-horizontal-scroll property is pinned, not assumed: the width budget is exported as named constants (896 / 32 / 864) and asserted against the declared 188 + (12 x 54) = 836, plus an assertion that no `overflow-x-auto` survives.  Two new registry invariants end the recurring "the registry omitted it" class.  `ComputerEngineCallout.test.ts` needed NO change ‚Äî its source slice is untouched and still passes, so every claim it protects survives.  Gate: `pnpm typecheck` exit 0; the 5 files this change touches pass 75/75.  The full suite reports one failure in `server/branching.test.ts` that is NOT from this change ‚Äî the diff touches zero `server/` files and the test passes 5/5 in isolation.  Housekeeping: while this lane was open, unrelated in-flight work from another writer (companion-tunnel typing in `SettingsModal.tsx`, `ConversationModeRow` payload hardening, and an empty-interface `satisfies` refactor that actually broke the build) landed in this worktree uncommitted.  It was saved to `~/apps/botfleet-mm-engines-matrix-scratch/foreign-writer-work.patch` rather than destroyed, excluded from this PR, and filed as so it is not lost.
- **2026-09-27 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî COMPLETED (PR #686 merged `7eee57d50`) ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] mcode ships a frozen two-model catalog ‚Äî MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview is unreachable (branch `minimax/mcode-model-catalog`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-mm-mcode`).**  Owner ask: run MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview inside mcode.  Two real defects, both found by driving the installed 0.5.5 CLI rather than reading code.  First: mcode advertises its no-variant selection as `m:minimax:MiniMax-M3:v:` ‚Äî a bare `v:` with an EMPTY variant ‚Äî while `parseMcodeModelValue` accepted only the `u` suffix, so every no-variant value decoded to null, nothing matched, and plain `MiniMax-M3` was unselectable on any login.  Second: the catalog listed `MiniMax-M3` and `MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed`, neither of which a session advertises, and a row the session does not advertise fails the turn.  The shipped catalog is now the advertised set read off a live session and folded through `mcodePickerId`, with a test asserting every shipped row resolves against it.  `resolveModels` (the core's existing hook, used the same way by dsh) reads `defaultModel` and `defaultModelVariant` from the CLI's own `config.yaml` and adopts that as the default; it deliberately appends no rows, because a pre-session id cannot be verified.  Upstream quirk documented rather than papered over: 0.5.5 advertises the flash preview's no-variant value and then fails it on a real turn with an opaque `Internal error` (2 of 2 attempts), while its `v:thinking` sibling answers, so the catalog offers the working row.  Live proof: discovery reads a real `config.yaml`, returns `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview-thinking` as the default, a driver boot adopts it before any session, and a real turn answers `FLASH-OK`.  Gate: typecheck clean, 207 tests passing, all CI checks green.  Found and deliberately NOT fixed here: `~/.local/bin/mcode` is a symlink to a launcher that is not symlink-safe (it resolves its data dir from the parent of `$0`, so through the symlink it looks for `~/.local/current` and exits non-zero); a GUI-launched BotFleet inherits a minimal PATH, so which candidate `resolveCli` lands on decides whether the engine launches at all.  That belongs in a follow-up with its own verification.  - **2026-09-27 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî COMPLETED (PR #682 merged `44a10bc86`) ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] Wire mcode as a fully supported CLI Engine (`mcodeAgent`) end to end (branch `minimax/mcode-cli-engine`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-mm-mcode`).**  Owner ask: connect mcode as a fully supported CLI Engine in BotFleet.  PR #679 landed the ACP driver (`server/drivers/acp/mcode.ts`) and registered it in `BUILT_IN_DRIVERS`, but nothing downstream knew the engine existed ‚Äî no capability row, no fleet instance, no quota mapping, no provider mark, no plan presets, no iOS mark, no docs row.  This change finishes the product surface.  mcode is a DISTINCT engine (id `mcode`, driver kind `mcodeAgent`, display name "MiniMax Code") that shares the MiniMax Token Plan and brand mark with the direct `minimax` engine, so it gets its own matrix row and badge one shade off MiniMax violet rather than being folded into an alias.  Server: `DEFAULT_FLEET` + `PRODUCT_FLEET_ADDITIONS` gain `mcode` so the engine exists on a fresh install and in an existing product config; `RESERVED_INSTANCE_ID` in `server/harness/registry.ts` gains `["mcodeAgent","mcode"]` so the default-fleet row is not misread as operator-added and offered a delete button; `driverKindsForWindow` now returns `mcodeAgent` for a MiniMax window and `quotaProviderForDriver("mcodeAgent")` answers `minimax`, which is what makes a mcode instance cappable and able to show a quota bar at all (deliberately NOT given an `ENGINE_METER_NOTES` row ‚Äî that vocabulary is for engines with no window, and claiming one would contradict the window it just gained); `PROVIDER_BY_ENGINE` and `GEN_AI_PROVIDERS` map the driver kind onto `minimax` so Usage Monitor and Sentry bucket the spend correctly.  Renderer: full `mcode` entry in `src/lib/engine-capabilities.tsx` (files, terminal, this computer, web, image attachments, connected apps, cross-bot coordination, long context ‚Äî each earned from the driver declaring `images: true` and the ACP core mounting MCP servers, which is why `connectedApps` is `yes` here and `no` on the direct MiniMax engine), plus `ENGINE_DISPLAY_ORDER`, `ENGINE_PLAN_OPTIONS`, `ProviderMark` (renders the MiniMax mark), and the two callout driver-kind lists.  iOS gets the MiniMax mark plus its own `MiniMax Code` VoiceOver label; the providers doc gets a built-in engines row.  NOT wired on purpose: `AUTO_FALLBACK_PRIORITY` is left alone because the ladder is instance-id keyed and mcode spends the same Token Plan as `minimax` ‚Äî a second rung would double-count one wallet.  Usage attribution rides the engine-metadata path (`bucketEngine` ‚Üí `engineIdFromDriverKind("mcodeAgent")` ‚Üí `mcode`), so the shared `includes("minimax")` model-name heuristic in the legacy-bucket path was deliberately left untouched rather than risk re-attributing existing MiniMax history.  `MiniMax-M3` is listed under both engines, so `uniqueModelToEngineId()` correctly drops it as ambiguous instead of crediting one engine.  No context figure is claimed for the M2.7 highspeed tier because the CLI does not publish one.  LANDED #682 at 4:24pm Central, 21 files / +169.  Gate: pnpm typecheck clean; full test floor 5917 passed / 19 skipped across 405 files (5936 counted vs a floor of 1070); sub-suites 258 passed / 0 failed; all 12 CI checks green including Swift + iOS build and Seer review.  Live roundtrip closed PR #679's open gap ‚Äî mcode 0.5.5 is installed on this Mac, the snapshot reported available and authenticated, the catalog returned MiniMax-M3 and MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed, and a real turn completed and answered as asked.  A lesson covering the full engine-wiring surface was contributed to fleet recall.

Verified all eleven of its PRs merged with `gh pr view` before closing either board, then closed a board item and a board item with the full PR list and moved both CLAUDE effort rows to COMPLETED. Separately diagnosed four live problems. (1) The "manual first adoption" refusal was a mis-report: the harness had crashed with a native V8 stack trace, leaving `harness-owner.json` naming a dead pid, and `readOwner` collapsed "no record" and "record for a dead process" into one null, so a crashed app was announced as a novel one-time adoption procedure. PR #798 adds `ownerRecordState`, which classifies the record as absent, stale or live, and reports a stale record as a harness that is not running and should be started; a genuinely absent record still gets the first-adoption text, so a real first adoption is unchanged. Behavioural test covers both branches, 116/116 on `pnpm test:mac-updater`. (2) Load average 780 was not BotFleet: an iOS simulator named `Harness-Claude-iPhone` had been booted 1 day 5 hours by the out-of-quota seat and was running 262 of the machine's 1250 processes. Shut it down without deleting it, which dropped load 780 to 82 and let the in-flight update build past `tsc`. (3) The app and the always-on the always-on harness LaunchAgent were both down, so the owner's whole bot system was offline; relaunched the app and confirmed health 200 with harness pid 41211. (4) The updater contention in the handoff was self-inflicted: pid 42253 was CLAUDE's own run, and the 2:13 AM refusal at the lock was a non-interactive agent call, so no shell history names it. That id is a hardcoded row in BotFleet's own `STATIC_DSH_MODELS`, and its comment states it is the canonical DSH-hosted MiniMax row per an explicit product decision, so excluding it would have silently undone that decision. The actual cause was runtime config: `~/.dsh/settings.yaml` declared the minimax provider with only M2.7, M2.7-highspeed and M3, and `subagent-model-selection.allowedModels` likewise omitted it, so the bridge really did not offer it. PR #682 had added the canonical dir to `knownDirs()` but placed it after `~/.local/bin`, so it changed nothing ‚Äî and since a GUI-launched BotFleet inherits a PATH with no mcode in it, `knownDirs` order is the only thing that decides the candidate. The test that should have caught it asserted the literal string ‚ÄúEmpty uses 12‚Äù was PRESENT in two files, which passes just as happily on a wrong number as a right one. Fixed structurally: the hard stop derives from the shared constant, the caption is generated by `toolRoundsCaption()`, and `loop.test.ts` pins the agreement between the number promised and the number enforced. Unset default therefore moves 12 ‚Üí 40 (owner-visible change; one constant to revert). **(2) The control vanished whenever the engine lookup had not answered** ‚Äî the gate was `engine?.capabilities?.toolLoop === true`, where `undefined` reads as ‚Äúno‚Äù, so it disappeared while `/api/instances` was in flight and for any engine the client had never heard of; only minimax, grok and openai-compat declare `toolLoop` at all. Nothing distinguished ‚Äúengine ignores this‚Äù from ‚Äúyou never set one‚Äù, so a ceiling saved on MiniMax stayed on the record invisible, inert and un-clearable. New `src/lib/bot-settings-gates.ts` is the single decision point: a saved value stays visible read-only with the reason, an unknown engine is never told ‚Äúno‚Äù, a fallback rollover names the engine actually running, and every capability row reads `botCapabilityGates()` once. iOS gets the same rule. **Testing** ‚Äî the string-presence assertions are replaced by real behaviour: gate unit tests, `renderToStaticMarkup` render tests (the repo‚Äôs DOM-free pattern), and a server test that the number promised equals the number enforced. **Verification** ‚Äî `pnpm typecheck` clean; 135 tests across the 6 affected files; `cd ios && swift test` 446 tests 0 failures; CI `lint` green and the diff adds zero violations (net ‚àí3) against a clean `origin/main` worktree. **Also found and filed** ‚Äî a set of board items: the per-rule lint baseline is 81 warnings stale versus main (4100 vs 4019), so the main-push lint job is red. First filed P0 on the wrong premise that it blocked PRs; corrected in a board comment after reading `node-ci.yml` ‚Äî PRs run `lint-pr-gate.mjs` (errors in touched files only) and the full ratchet is a non-PR gate. 

- **2026-09-27 ‚Äî MINIMAX ‚Äî IN PR (PR #691, auto-merge armed) ‚Äî [BF][MINIMAX] Redesign the Engines settings capability matrix: whole table visible, readable cell detail, no hidden engines (a set of board items,, worktree ` Owner ask, verbatim: "thoroughly redesign and improve the bot engines settings capability matrix and capability definitions, since the way it is now you can't see the whole table ever, and mousing over certain columns shows you text below that can't even mostly see, so it is just not working as intended ‚Äî and also needed further improvement even if it was working as intended." Four real defects, all confirmed against source before implementation. (1) The table could NEVER fit: the dialog caps at 1100px, the `shrink-0` nav eats 190px, the pane adds a 40px gutter, so the content pane offers 870px, while the table declared 160px + (8 x 120px) = 1120px; the first column was `sticky left-0`, so roughly 5.9 of 8 engines were ever on screen and the rest was a horizontal scroll nobody found. mcode (PR #682) made it worse by adding an eighth engine column. (2) The hover explanation rendered AFTER the table at the bottom of the card ‚Äî a 12-row table ends ~570-600px into a ~650px viewport, so the block began at or past the fold, and `onMouseLeave` cleared the state, so scrolling toward it destroyed it. That is exactly the "text below that can't even mostly see". (3) Engines that are not installed were not rendered AT ALL: `showDisabled` defaulted to `false`, so every instance failing `isEngineEnabled` sat behind a `Show (N)` count. A content bug, not a layout bug, and it survived every resize. (4) Missing registry keys masqueraded as "no": `capabilities` is `Partial<Record<...>>` and the matrix resolved a missing key through `CELL_TONE[state ?? ""] ?? CELL_TONE.no` while `capabilityCellLabel(undefined)` returned the `‚Äî` glyph, so an unaudited pair rendered as a dash wearing the not-available colour. The source comments show this class has bitten at least four separate times. Fix: the matrix is TRANSPOSED (engines are rows, capabilities are columns, grouped into four named categories), which both fits and lines up with the engine rail directly below it. The detail strip now LATCHES ‚Äî always rendered at a fixed `min-height`, takes the last hovered OR focused cell, and never clears on `mouseleave`, so hovering cannot shift the layout under the pointer and focus latches too for keyboard use. `unknown` became a real verdict with its own dashed tone, and 13 unaudited pairs are flagged honestly rather than asserted either way. `CAPABILITY_NOTES` plus a per-engine override now explain what each capability actually IS in BotFleet, so the strip teaches instead of re-reading the engine's pitch. `showDisabled` defaults to `true` and each row reports the real availability reason from fields the runtime actually provides. Dialog cap 720px -> 880px, nav 190px -> 164px. The no-horizontal-scroll property is pinned, not assumed: the width budget is exported as named constants (896 / 32 / 864) and asserted against the declared 188 + (12 x 54) = 836, plus an assertion that no `overflow-x-auto` survives. Two new registry invariants end the recurring "the registry omitted it" class. `ComputerEngineCallout.test.ts` needed NO change ‚Äî its source slice is untouched and still passes, so every claim it protects survives. Gate: `pnpm typecheck` exit 0; the 5 files this change touches pass 75/75. The full suite reports one failure in `server/branching.test.ts` that is NOT from this change ‚Äî the diff touches zero `server/` files and the test passes 5/5 in isolation. Housekeeping: while this lane was open, unrelated in-flight work from another writer (companion-tunnel typing in `SettingsModal.tsx`, `ConversationModeRow` payload hardening, and an empty-interface `satisfies` refactor that actually broke the build) landed in this worktree uncommitted. It was saved to ` rather than destroyed, excluded from this PR, and filed as a set of board items so it is not lost.

First: mcode advertises its no-variant selection as `m:minimax:MiniMax-M3:v:` ‚Äî a bare `v:` with an EMPTY variant ‚Äî while `parseMcodeModelValue` accepted only the `u` suffix, so every no-variant value decoded to null, nothing matched, and plain `MiniMax-M3` was unselectable on any login. Gate: typecheck clean, 207 tests passing, all CI checks green. PR #679 landed the ACP driver (`server/drivers/acp/mcode.ts`) and registered it in `BUILT_IN_DRIVERS`, but nothing downstream knew the engine existed ‚Äî no capability row, no fleet instance, no quota mapping, no provider mark, no plan presets, no iOS mark, no docs row. This change finishes the product surface. mcode is a DISTINCT engine (id `mcode`, driver kind `mcodeAgent`, display name "MiniMax Code") that shares the MiniMax Token Plan and brand mark with the direct `minimax` engine, so it gets its own matrix row and badge one shade off MiniMax violet rather than being folded into an alias. Server: `DEFAULT_FLEET` + `PRODUCT_FLEET_ADDITIONS` gain `mcode` so the engine exists on a fresh install and in an existing product config; `RESERVED_INSTANCE_ID` in `server/harness/registry.ts` gains `["mcodeAgent","mcode"]` so the default-fleet row is not misread as operator-added and offered a delete button; `driverKindsForWindow` now returns `mcodeAgent` for a MiniMax window and `quotaProviderForDriver("mcodeAgent")` answers `minimax`, which is what makes a mcode instance cappable and able to show a quota bar at all (deliberately NOT given an `ENGINE_METER_NOTES` row ‚Äî that vocabulary is for engines with no window, and claiming one would contradict the window it just gained); `PROVIDER_BY_ENGINE` and `GEN_AI_PROVIDERS` map the driver kind onto `minimax` so Usage Monitor and Sentry bucket the spend correctly. Renderer: full `mcode` entry in `src/lib/engine-capabilities.tsx` (files, terminal, this computer, web, image attachments, connected apps, cross-bot coordination, long context ‚Äî each earned from the driver declaring `images: true` and the ACP core mounting MCP servers, which is why `connectedApps` is `yes` here and `no` on the direct MiniMax engine), plus `ENGINE_DISPLAY_ORDER`, `ENGINE_PLAN_OPTIONS`, `ProviderMark` (renders the MiniMax mark), and the two callout driver-kind lists. iOS gets the MiniMax mark plus its own `MiniMax Code` VoiceOver label; the providers doc gets a built-in engines row. NOT wired on purpose: `AUTO_FALLBACK_PRIORITY` is left alone because the ladder is instance-id keyed and mcode spends the same Token Plan as `minimax` ‚Äî a second rung would double-count one wallet. Usage attribution rides the engine-metadata path (`bucketEngine` ‚Üí `engineIdFromDriverKind("mcodeAgent")` ‚Üí `mcode`), so the shared `includes("minimax")` model-name heuristic in the legacy-bucket path was deliberately left untouched rather than risk re-attributing existing MiniMax history. `MiniMax-M3` is listed under both engines, so `uniqueModelToEngineId()` correctly drops it as ambiguous instead of crediting one engine. No context figure is claimed for the M2.7 highspeed tier because the CLI does not publish one. LANDED #682 at 4:24pm Central, 21 files / +169. Gate: pnpm typecheck clean; full test floor 5917 passed / 19 skipped across 405 files (5936 counted vs a floor of 1070); sub-suites 258 passed / 0 failed; all 12 CI checks green including Swift + iOS build and Seer review. Live roundtrip closed PR #679's open gap ‚Äî mcode 0.5.5 is installed on this Mac, the snapshot reported available and authenticated, the catalog returned MiniMax-M3 and MiniMax-M2.7-highspeed, and a real turn completed and answered as asked. A lesson covering the full engine-wiring surface was contributed to fleet recall.

New `server/grok-quota.ts` mirrors `server/antigravity-quota.ts`; today the `grok` CLI has no quota subcommand, so the poller returns an honest `method: "no-source"` stub with a populated reason ‚Äî the UI surfaces a "no quota source available yet" line.  Tests added for every new file (38/38 passing across the 6 new/updated vitest suites after `vite.config.ts` was extended to include `src/**/*.test.tsx`; `pnpm typecheck` clean).  Audit doc at `docs/audits/2026-09-23-botfleet-settings-revamp.md`.  Deviations: (a) Vercel preview deployment still rate-limited (`api-deployments-free-per-day`) ‚Äî not a required status check so not blocking; (b) `~/Code/Usage-Monitor` mirror not shipped this round per task brief "Mirror is fine ‚Äî Usage Monitor has its own repo workflow"; (c) vitest pick-up surfaced a pre-existing gap ‚Äî component `.test.tsx` files had been silently excluded by `vite.config.ts` include, fixed by adding the pattern so CI's test-floor wrapper actually counts and runs them (EngineCallout's HTML-entity assertion also fixed in the same commit `33792c72`).
- **2026-09-23 ‚Äî MM ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BotFleet][MM] Computer Use settings redesign: per-provider toggles + impact-confirm + matrix (board `9613f135` / `660f5bf2`, branch `minimax/computer-use-settings-redesign`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-mm-computer-use-settings`).**  Replace the three legacy toggles in the Computer settings section with four per-provider `<ComputerProviderToggle>` rows (ASCII.dev Box, Self-Hosted VPS, Local VM, This Computer) and a `<VpsModeToggle>` (Shared / Per-Bot / Not Used).  Disabling a provider that any bot currently uses opens a `<ComputerImpactConfirmModal>` listing every bot that would lose a leg of its grant, with "Disable anyway" (accent red) and "Cancel" (neutral).  A `<BotComputerMatrix>` row-per-bot table shows the per-provider grant for every bot, with a header "Apply new default to all" button.  Schema migration in `shared/local-auto-consent.ts` adds `ComputerProviders` + `VpsMode`; `migrateComputerProvidersConfig` runs in `loadConfig()` and `migrateComputerProviders` runs in `electron/main.mjs`'s boot, both idempotent.  Local VM runtime setup is preserved in `<LocalVmRuntimeCard>` so the redesigned section does not silently drop the one-click container install.  Audit at `docs/audits/2026-09-21-computer-use-settings-redesign.md`.  `pnpm typecheck` clean; 22 new tests in `src/lib/computer-providers.test.ts` (22/22 pass); 5 existing `shared/local-auto-consent.test.ts` pass; 115 `server/config.test.ts` pass; 45 `server/computer-grants.test.ts` pass; 53 `src/state/store.test.ts` pass; 3 `LocalComputerAutoWarning.test.ts` pass.
- **2026-09-21 ‚Äî AG ‚Äî COMPLETED (Harness PR #9 merged `b824d66`, BotFleet PR #518 merged `2b6b907b`) ‚Äî Fix DSH MiniMax model option serialization (provider minimax) (board `41b6134a`, branch `ag/dsh-minimax-model-option`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-ag-dsh-minimax`).**  In Simple-With-Us/Clutch, updated `dshModelOptionValue` and `dshModelIdFromOptionValue` to serialize and deserialize MiniMax models under provider `minimax` (e.g. `["minimax","MiniMax-M3"]`) instead of hardcoding `deepseek-official`, fixing DSH ACP `session/set_config_option` rejection on model switch.  Bumped `harness` dependency in BotFleet, re-exported `DSH_MINIMAX_PROVIDER_ID` and `dshProviderForModel` from `server/drivers/acp/dsh.ts`, and added test assertions for round-trip encoding/decoding.  Landed in Harness PR #9 and BotFleet PR #518, deployed to production via `update-botfleet.sh`.
- **2026-09-21 ‚Äî AG ‚Äî COMPLETED (PR #517 merged `a3ec130b`) ‚Äî Fix Computer Use toggle selection highlight, Settings navigation label, and VPS setup hint (board `ce2e4194` / `31636bd1` / `bbe48a9a`, branch `ag/computer-use-settings-and-design`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-ag-computer-settings`).**  Fixes light-mode invisible selection on Self-hosted VPS segmented control by changing `bg-raised` to `bg-control font-medium` across `BotComputerDefaults` and `CloudBackendPicker`.  Relabels Settings nav section from 'Local VM' to 'Computers' with comprehensive search keywords.  Adds inline VPS setup guidance when SSH host alias is missing.  Resolves sentence gap spacing across Computer Use settings copy.  Landed in PR #517 and deployed via `update-botfleet.sh`.
- **2026-09-21 ‚Äî AG ‚Äî IN PR #516 (auto-merge armed) ‚Äî Fix Antigravity host policy refusal and unblock model fallback (board `2a9a509b`, branch `ag/platform-capabilities-and-fallbacks`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-ag-platform-capabilities`).**  Honors user auto-approval (`turn.autoApprove` or `config.fullAuto`) on host control turns so tools run smoothly under `always-proceed` without false policy blocks.  Fixes fallback suppression in `turnProducedAssistantOutput` where the `ANTIGRAVITY_HOST_CONTROL_NOTICE` chip was marked `ok: true` and treated as assistant tool output, unblocking failover to secondary engines.  Degrades host computer cleanly on auto-fallback when interactive asks cannot be brokered.
- **2026-09-21 ‚Äî AG ‚Äî COMPLETED (PR #514 merged `3e9f65d0`) ‚Äî iOS companion: 'Open BotFleet on Mac' action; stop bot cancel/snooze loop & pause/resume across updates (board `88d92756` / `9c03e8cb`, branch `ag/ios-open-desktop-on-mac`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-ag-open-mac`).**  Added client and session methods for `POST /api/desktop/open` to let companion request opening or unhiding BotFleet desktop app on the paired Mac.  Update `ChatListView` StatusBanner with actionable 'Open BotFleet on Mac' button alongside offline diagnostic.  Fixed bot stop/interrupt loop: atomic cancellation of all queued/running/waiting runs for a bot and snoozing background triggers until explicit user action, preventing loops where Compiler or other bots restart 5 seconds later.  Enhanced updater and runtime quiesce to allow update initiation while busy: snapshots in-flight work to `pending-update-resume.json`, quiesces turns, and automatically clears snoozes and requeues runs on harness reboot on the updated build.
- **2026-09-19 ‚Äî MM ‚Äî IN PR ‚Äî [BotFleet][MM] engines polish: collapsible MiniMax callout, drop MM-M2.7 from DSH, Codex default ‚Üí Luna (board `b5953d96`, branch `mm/engine-polish`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-mm-engine-polish`).** MiniMax callout text duplicates across EnginesSettings.tsx and ModelPicker.tsx, pushing model names off-screen; extracted to a shared collapsible `src/components/MiniMaxCallout.tsx` with a "Why this engine?" disclosure while pointing to DeepSeek Harness + MiniMax M3 for full tool support. Dropped MiniMax-M2.7 from STATIC_DSH_MODELS in `server/drivers/acp/dsh.ts` (M3 dominates on context and cost). Updated STATIC_CODEX_MODELS default to `gpt-5.6-luna` in `server/drivers/codex-catalog.ts`. Tests updated across `dsh.test.ts`, `codex-catalog.test.ts`, and `MiniMaxCallout.test.ts` (48/48 green, `pnpm typecheck` clean).
- **2026-09-19 ‚Äî MM ‚Äî COMPLETED (PR #481 merged `c1bf6aa8`) ‚Äî quota dual-window: route non-MiniMax engines through DRIVER_KIND_PROVIDERS (board ebecfe8e, branch `ag/model-picker-dual-quota`, worktree `~/apps/botfleet-mm-quota-fix`).**  AG's lane, CRITICAL Seer thread `PRRT_kwDOUHUvas6j6pZT`: `server/harness/registry.ts:418` was building `providerKey = inst.driverKind === "openai" ?
